Phase 34 (Tauri packaging — Windows/Linux/macOS bundles, signing plumbing, minisign auto-update). Minted 2026-07-24 during `/gsd-plan-phase 34` from 34-CONTEXT.md D-01..D-09 + 34-RESEARCH.md "Phase Requirements" boundary table (ROADMAP read `TBD — mint at /gsd-plan-phase 34`). REQ-34-07 is the keyring cross-platform feature gap surfaced by research, not named in CONTEXT.md.

- [x] **REQ-34-01**: macOS productionization. Flip `src-tauri/tauri.conf.json` `bundle.active:false->true` and set `bundle.icon` to the committed `src-tauri/icons/` set; `dmg` is in `bundle.targets`; the macOS `.dmg` bundles and the additive/reversible invariant holds (both `npm start` and `npm run tauri:dev` still launch). No macOS packaging gap deferred to Phase 35. *(D-01)*
- [x] **REQ-34-02**: Windows + Linux bundle targets. `bundle.targets` is the explicit lean set `["nsis","appimage","dmg"]` (replacing `"all"`); NSIS (Windows) + AppImage (Linux) are the updater-eligible formats. Adding Linux `.deb`/`.rpm` is discretionary and, if added, is a manual-install convenience that does NOT auto-update via the Tauri feed. *(D-01, D-02)*
- [x] **REQ-34-03**: Sidecar single self-contained per-OS binary. The Phase 27 Node sidecar (`build/main/sidecar.js`) compiles via Node SEA legacy 2-step (`node --experimental-sea-config` + `postject`, NOT `--build-sea`) through a new `meta/buildSidecarSea.ts` + `build:sidecar-sea` script into `src-tauri/binaries/gamelib-sidecar-<triple>[.exe]`, wired as Tauri `externalBin`; `main.rs`'s `spawn_sidecar` resolves the packaged binary via `tauri-plugin-shell` (no external Node on the user machine) while preserving the `GAMELIB_SIDECAR_ENTRY` dev override + diagnostic logging. The `decompressPool` worker_threads path is an accepted single-thread throughput regression under the compiled binary (existing inline fallback). *(D-06)*
- [x] **REQ-34-04**: Signing/notarization plumbing with graceful skip. Signing creds read from env/GitHub secrets; the base `tauri.conf.json` declares NO `certificateThumbprint`/`signCommand`. macOS skips signing natively when `APPLE_*` secrets are absent; the Windows CI leg gates an explicit `--config` signing override behind BOTH `env.WINDOWS_CERTIFICATE != ''` AND `env.WINDOWS_CERT_THUMBPRINT != ''` (34-15/GAP-4 closed the half-enrolled-secret hard-fail), warn-and-skips otherwise, so a secrets-less OR half-configured run ships a working UNSIGNED artifact and the CI job NEVER fails on missing certs. Code-level static half closed; live tag-push proof remains bundled into 34-07's deferred gate. *(D-03, D-04)*
- [x] **REQ-34-05**: Updater plugin + minisign keypair + fork-pointed feed. A minisign keypair is generated this phase (`tauri signer generate`); the PUBLIC key is committed into `tauri.conf.json` `plugins.updater.pubkey`, the PRIVATE key + password live ONLY as GitHub Actions secrets. `plugins.updater.endpoints` is HARDCODED to `https://github.com/grayson-mitchell/GameLib/releases/download/updater/latest.json` — a fixed-tag asset location a draft+prerelease-only release pipeline can actually serve (GitHub's `/releases/latest/download/` form resolves only to non-prerelease, non-draft releases and permanently 404s under D-09's locked `prerelease: true`; corrected in 34-14 gap closure, GAP-3) — never derived from `package.json.repository` (still Heroic by design) — with a negative test asserting `Heroic-Games-Launcher` never appears in the feed config. `bundle.createUpdaterArtifacts:true`; `tauri-plugin-updater`/`@tauri-apps/plugin-updater` 2.10.1 + `updater:default` capability. *(D-07, D-08)*
- [x] **REQ-34-06**: CI release pipeline. A new `.github/workflows/release-tauri.yml` builds a `windows-latest`+`ubuntu-latest`+`macos-latest` (incl. mac arm64 & x64) matrix via `tauri-apps/tauri-action`, triggered on `v*` tag push (+ `workflow_dispatch`), producing a DRAFT GitHub Release marked PRERELEASE with per-platform artifacts + a minisign-signed `latest.json`, reusing the repo's `./.github/actions/install-deps` composite. Co-triggering with the existing Electron `draft-release-*` workflows on `v*` is accepted; artifact filenames must not collide. *(D-05, D-09)*
- [x] **REQ-34-07**: keyring crate cross-platform features. `src-tauri/Cargo.toml`'s `keyring` dependency adds `windows-native` + `sync-secret-service` alongside the existing `apple-native` (no major-version bump) so OS-keychain Steam-token persistence works on Windows/Linux, not just macOS; target GNOME/KDE Secret Service on Linux (runtime "no keyring found" surfaces as the existing structured `keyring:unavailable` error, not a crash). *(implied by D-01; surfaced by 34-RESEARCH.md)*
- [x] **REQ-34-08**: Additive/reversible invariant. After flipping `bundle.active:true` and adding the updater/shell plugins, BOTH `npm start` (Electron) and `npm run tauri:dev` still launch unchanged; no `src/backend/sidecar/` file imports real `electron`; `electronUntouched.test.ts` stays green; the Tauri shell `cargo build` still succeeds on macOS with the expanded keyring features. *(hard constraint / SEAM invariant)*
- [x] **REQ-34-09**: Live phase-close gate (Manual-Only). A real `v*` test-tag push proves all three matrix legs complete, a Draft+prerelease Release appears with the 3 platform artifacts + `latest.json`, it is NOT marked GitHub "Latest" and is invisible to the updater until manual publish (Phase 19 lesson), signing is gracefully skipped (unsigned artifacts + warning, job green), and the compiled sidecar runs standalone with no system Node on PATH. Jest cannot exercise a GitHub Actions run or a Node-free container — this is a `checkpoint:human-verify` gate. *(D-04, D-09)* — **PASSED live 2026-07-25, run [30123449346](https://github.com/grayson-mitchell/GameLib/actions/runs/30123449346) on commit 006a900a: 4/4 legs green, draft+prerelease release with all artifacts + latest.json, macOS+Windows signing gracefully skipped, arm64 SEA sidecar ran Node-free. See 34-HUMAN-UAT.md.**

Phase 34.1 (Tauri IPC re-plumb slice 4 — app shell and window chrome). Minted 2026-07-25 during `/gsd-plan-phase 34.1` from 34.1-CONTEXT.md D-01..D-15 + 34.1-RESEARCH.md "Phase Requirements" area table (ROADMAP read `TBD — mint at /gsd-plan-phase 34.1`). The slice is **33 channels, not 34** — D-14 reassigns `callTool` to Phase 34.5.

- [x] **REQ-34.1-01**: Window chrome executes RENDERER-SIDE and counts as ported. The ten channels `minimizeWindow`/`maximizeWindow`/`unmaximizeWindow`/`closeWindow`/`isMaximized`/`isMinimized`/`isFullscreen`/`setFullscreen`/`isFrameless`/`setZoomFactor` are implemented in `src/preload/api/` behind an `isTauri()` short-circuit against `getCurrentWindow()` from `@tauri-apps/api/window` (nine of them) and `getCurrentWebview().setZoom()` from `@tauri-apps/api/webview` (`setZoomFactor` only — zoom is a **webview**-scoped API in Tauri v2, not window-scoped). No sidecar hop, no new `dispatch_rust_channel` arms, and the sidecar registers NOTHING for these ten (D-02: a sidecar registration would be unreachable dead code behind the preload short-circuit). `isFullscreen` reproduces Electron's `isSteamDeckGameMode || isCLIFullscreen` env/CLI semantics honestly (declared-false under Tauri, mirroring `tauriAttach.ts`'s existing `isSteamDeckGameMode = false` stub) and MUST NOT be substituted with a real `getCurrentWindow().isFullscreen()` query. Each replaced export keeps its exported name and call signature byte-identical so `common/types/ipc.ts`'s `AsyncIPCFunctions`/`SyncIPCFunctions` contract keeps type-checking; the Electron path keeps flowing through the existing `makeHandlerInvoker`/`makeListenerCaller` factories unchanged. *(D-01, D-02)*
- [x] **REQ-34.1-02**: Capability grants are an explicit per-command allow-list, composition verified first. `src-tauri/capabilities/default.json` gains exactly thirteen identifiers and nothing broader: `core:window:allow-minimize`, `allow-maximize`, `allow-toggle-maximize`, `allow-unmaximize`, `allow-close`, `allow-start-dragging`, `allow-set-fullscreen`, `allow-is-fullscreen`, `allow-is-maximized`, `allow-is-minimized`, `allow-set-decorations`, `allow-set-title-bar-style`, plus `core:webview:allow-set-webview-zoom`. `core:window:default`'s real composition is verified against the installed tauri 2.11.5 crate's own generated permission reference (it is a read-only getter set containing none of the mutation commands) and that verification is cited in the plan rather than taken on recollection; `core:window:default` is NOT granted wholesale. The file's top-level `description` is extended in its existing minimal-exposure prose style explaining why each grant is explicit — load-bearing because capabilities are scoped `"windows": ["main"]` and GameLib renders untrusted remote content in that window with no `<webview>` isolation boundary under Tauri. *(D-04)*
  - **Amendment 2026-08-13 (G4 gap cycle 1, plan 34.1-10):** the thirteenth grant, `core:window:allow-set-title-bar-style`, was added because `setTitleBarStyle` is the macOS decoration mechanism `applyFramelessDecorations` now uses in place of `setDecorations` — `setDecorations(false)` strips the native traffic lights on macOS (gap G4 / UAT test 9), so it is not usable there. This is a widening of the identifier count from twelve to thirteen, not a change to this requirement's reasoning or its `core:window:default` verification clause.
- [x] **REQ-34.1-03**: `framelessWindow` is honored at RUNTIME; the default stays decorated. Framelessness is an off-by-default user **setting** (`settings.framelessWindow`), not a platform property — "match Electron" means make the setting work, not ship frameless. The renderer reads the setting and calls `getCurrentWindow().setDecorations(!frameless)` before first paint (from the `tauriAttach.ts` attach path, so the window never visibly flips), and re-applies when the setting is toggled. When frameless is ON, `decorations: false` on ALL platforms and GameLib's own titlebar buttons render on all three — one code path, no per-platform branch — because `navigator.windowControlsOverlay` is undefined under Tauri (already guarded, Phase 33 gap-fix #3), so `App.tsx`'s `showOverlayControls = isFrameless && !hasNativeOverlayControls` evaluates true everywhere with ZERO frontend change. This diverges from Electron on macOS/Windows (native overlay, own buttons hidden) and that divergence is **declared, not discovered**. *(D-05, D-06)*
  - **Supersession/amendment, dated 2026-08-14 (gap cycle 1, G4, plans 34.1-10/34.1-11, live-confirmed by 34.1-14):** two live UAT sessions found the "one code path, no per-platform branch" clause above did not survive contact with a real macOS window. Recorded here as two SEPARATE notes, because the two halves of the original text were falsified differently and conflating them would write a false constraint into the permanent record:
    - **SUPERSEDED on macOS:** the clause "GameLib's own titlebar buttons render on all three platforms, one code path, no per-platform branch." On macOS the OS draws the controls (native traffic lights); GameLib's own `WindowControls` no longer renders there at all, gated unconditionally by plan 34.1-11's `isMac` check. Windows/Linux are unaffected — `WindowControls` still renders there under the original one-code-path logic.
    - **AMENDED IN MECHANISM, NOT IN INTENT:** the clause "the renderer reads the setting and calls `getCurrentWindow().setDecorations(!frameless)`." On macOS that call is replaced by `getCurrentWindow().setTitleBarStyle('overlay'|'visible')`, because `setDecorations(false)` was found to strip the native traffic lights entirely — not the Tauri equivalent of Electron's `titleBarStyle: 'hidden'`, which the original REQ text assumed. The requirement's actual INTENT — `framelessWindow` is honoured at RUNTIME and genuinely affects decorations, on every platform, with no restart required — is UPHELD, not superseded: plan 34.1-14 live-confirmed both directions work on macOS (L2/L5 both PASS) and neither transition flickers (L3/L6 both PASS). Windows/Linux keep `setDecorations`, unchanged.
    - This amendment does NOT mean, and must never be read to mean, that Tauri lacks a runtime `titleBarStyle` setter or that the macOS overlaid state is one-way/permanent — `setTitleBarStyle` exists (`@tauri-apps/api` 2.11.1) and was observed working in both directions on real hardware. An earlier planning revision asserted the opposite and was blocked by the plan-checker before it reached the operator; see `34.1-HUMAN-UAT.md`'s dedicated "Frameless-toggle-on-macOS decision (Section C)" section for the live-determined PATH A / A1 outcome this amendment reflects.
- [x] **REQ-34.1-04**: Handler bodies are EXTRACTED from `main.ts`, not re-implemented. The genuinely-portable handler bodies move into new Electron-free modules under `src/backend/appshell/` (themes, releases/changelog, language); `src/backend/main.ts` keeps its `addHandler`/`addListener` registration lines as one-line delegations so its registration site stays auditable in one place and its diff stays mechanical. Only ~10 of the 28 `main.ts` handlers are genuine body moves: the ten D-01 window-chrome handlers need NO extraction at all (renderer-side, their Electron bodies stay untouched), `lock`/`unlock`/`setTitleBarOverlay` are logged no-ops, and `gamepadAction`/`changeTrayColor`/`createNewWindow`/`showAboutWindow` are from-scratch Tauri work that extraction cannot shortcut. This is the first slice to modify the Electron entry point — the additive/reversible invariant is **BEHAVIORAL, not textual**, from here on. *(D-07, D-08)*
- [x] **REQ-34.1-05**: Curated sidecar registration for the 19 sidecar-routed channels. A new `src/backend/sidecar/appShellFlowRegistration.ts` registers `getCustomThemes`/`getThemeCSS`/`getCustomCSS`/`getHeroicVersion`/`getLatestReleases`/`getCurrentChangelog`/`changeLanguage`/`notify`/`quit`/`openReleases`/`openCustomThemesWiki`/`openWebviewPage`/`abort`/`isIntelMac`/`getWebviewPreloadPath`/`lock`/`unlock`/`setTitleBarOverlay` on `electronStub`'s `ipcMain`, wired from `handlers.ts` alongside the existing five `register*Flows()` calls. `set-connectivity-online` is already reachable via `initOnlineMonitor()` in `bootstrap.ts` (Phase 33) and is proven by test rather than re-registered — a second `ipcMain.on` would double-fire `setStatus`. `quit`/`openReleases`/`openCustomThemesWiki`/`openWebviewPage`/`notify` are thin re-wirings of the EXISTING `app_exit`/`open_external`/`notification_show` transport (**zero new Rust arms** — if any needs new Rust work, stop and ask why). Curated imports only: no file under `src/backend/sidecar/` imports the real `electron` module, and `utils/ipc_handler.ts` is NOT side-effect-imported for `abort`/`isIntelMac` (it also registers slice-6/8 channels) — `callAbortController` and `isIntelMac` are imported directly instead. The import-graph gate for this is a **genuinely new** by-construction test: `electronUntouched.test.ts` is Phase 28's keyring/`configStore` byte-identity proof and asserts nothing about `main.ts`'s textual state, so it must NOT be repurposed. *(D-03, D-09)*
- [x] **REQ-34.1-06**: `gamepadAction` is re-implemented RENDERER-SIDE. Electron's `webContents.sendInputEvent` has no Tauri equivalent, so the renderer dispatches its own synthetic DOM events / focus moves instead of round-tripping: directional actions (`padUp`/`padDown`/`padLeft`/`padRight`/`leftStickUp`/`leftStickDown`/`leftStickLeft`/`leftStickRight`) move focus by bounding-rect geometry rather than relying on Chromium spatial navigation (which WKWebView/WebView2 do not provide); `tab`/`shiftTab` traverse the tabbable list; `esc` dispatches a real `Escape` KeyboardEvent; `back` calls `history.back()`; `leftClick`/`rightClick` hit-test `document.elementFromPoint(metadata.x, metadata.y)` and dispatch mouse/pointer events; `rightStickUp`/`rightStickDown` scroll the scrollable ancestor at window centre. Real consumers exist (`isSteamDeckGameMode`, the `/console` routes, `Dropdown`/`Dialog` tab calls), so a logged no-op would silently remove a shipped feature. *(D-10)*
- [x] **REQ-34.1-07**: `changeTrayColor` acts on a REAL Tauri tray. Not a logged no-op — a colour-change channel is structurally hollow without a tray. `src-tauri/Cargo.toml` enables the `tray-icon` Cargo feature (currently `features = []`); `main.rs` builds a `TrayIcon` at setup with a tooltip, a left-click show/focus of the `main` window, and a minimal Show/Quit menu; a new `tray_set_icon` `rustInvoke` arm swaps between the dark and light icon variants embedded at compile time via `include_bytes!`. The sidecar's `changeTrayColor` registration reads `darkTrayIcon` from `GlobalConfig` and drives that arm, plus one initial sync at registration. **Explicitly bounded** (this is a FEATURE, not a port — the single largest scope risk in the slice): the recent-games submenu, per-game `gamelib://launch` protocol entries, the About/Reload/Debug menu items, the macOS dock menu, `languageChanged` menu rebuilds, and the `noTrayIcon`/`exitToTray` settings are **declared out of scope** and re-deferred to Phase 35. Tray control is Rust-side only, so NO `core:tray:*` capability is granted to the renderer (minimal exposure, consistent with Phase 33 WR-03). *(D-11)*
- [x] **REQ-34.1-08**: `createNewWindow` + `showAboutWindow` are genuine Tauri `WebviewWindow`s, and the child-window capability boundary is fail-closed. Both are implemented renderer-side via `new WebviewWindow(label, options)` from `@tauri-apps/api/webviewWindow` — this resolves 34.1-RESEARCH.md Open Question 1 explicitly: the constructor is a webview-context-only JS API that the headless Node sidecar structurally cannot call, and the alternative (a new Rust arm) is not covered by the zero-new-arms pre-approval. D-03's enumerated sidecar-seam list does not name these two channels, so this does not contradict it. `showAboutWindow` opens a static `about.html` shipped from `public/` (electron-vite copies `public/*` to `build/` = `frontendDist`) carrying the same fields Electron's `app.showAboutPanel()` showed. `core:webview:allow-create-webview-window` is granted to `main` only. Child window labels are **not** listed in any capability's `windows` array, so they inherit zero IPC surface — asserted by test, because `createNewWindow(url)` loads renderer-supplied remote content and there is no `<webview>` isolation boundary under Tauri. `openWebviewPage` routes to the existing `open_external` transport; `getWebviewPreloadPath` returns a declared-empty value; the `<webview>` login story (navigation interception, OAuth redirect capture, session/cookie access) stays Phase 34.4's. *(D-12)*
- [x] **REQ-34.1-09**: Carried-forward logged no-ops, declared and never silent. `lock`/`unlock` are `powerSaveBlocker` (`main.ts:610`/`625`) — already accepted as a logged no-op by Phase 33 D-08, carried forward unchanged through `electronStub`'s existing logging `powerSaveBlocker` stub and re-declared in `34.1-PORTED-CHANNELS.md` for completeness. `setTitleBarOverlay` (`main.ts:1541`) is a Windows/macOS-Electron API with no native overlay left to target under D-06 — a logged no-op naming that reason. Both log on every call and neither throws. *(D-13)*
- [x] **REQ-34.1-10**: `callTool` is reassigned to Phase 34.5 and the inventory reconciles. `tools/ipc_handler.ts:25` is Winetricks/winecfg/runExe — Wine tooling, 34.5's entire domain; it sits in slice 4 only because the inventory grouped channels by file, and porting it here would drag `runWineCommandOnGame` + `Winetricks` into this slice's import graph. `.planning/IPC-PORT-INVENTORY.md` is corrected in **both** lists — Phase 34.1 header 34 → 33 channels with `callTool` removed from its list, Phase 34.5 header 55 → 56 channels with `callTool` added in sorted position — so the Phase 35 completion gate stays reconcilable. *(D-14)*
- [x] **REQ-34.1-11**: `34.1-PORTED-CHANNELS.md` declares all 33 rows plus the honest deferred-UAT statement, and SEAM.md §3 rows move to §1 (Incremental-Port Checklist steps 5/6). The artifact declares a **third port kind** — `renderer-side (Tauri JS)` — alongside the existing `sidecar invoke/send` and `rustInvoke` kinds, and enumerates every logged no-op, declared-empty return, and re-deferral (tray menu depth, `noTrayIcon`/`exitToTray`, login-webview plumbing, `powerSaveBlocker` parity, `setTitleBarOverlay` real behavior, `getLatestReleases`/`openReleases` still pointing at `heroicGithubURL`). **D-15's honest tension must be recorded verbatim, not softened:** this slice's headline deliverable is visual and interactive — "the frameless toggle applies," "the buttons actually move the window," "the tray appears and its colour changes," "gamepad navigation still works," "macOS traffic lights vs custom buttons looks right" — and jest structurally cannot prove any of it. Every automated test in this phase proves *wiring*: that the right Tauri API was called with the right arguments. **None proves the window on screen actually did the thing. The phase closes without anyone having looked at it.** No downstream reader may read "wired and unit-proven" as "the window chrome was seen working" — that is the exact claim class that let G-30-02 be declared fixed twice while the live build hung. *(D-02, D-15, checklist steps 5/6)*
- [x] **REQ-34.1-12**: The additive/reversible invariant holds BEHAVIORALLY. `npm start` (Electron) and `pnpm tauri:dev` both still work after every plan, with zero change to the Electron build's observable behavior — but unlike every prior slice this one **does** modify `src/backend/main.ts` by design (D-07), so the invariant is behavioral, not textual. `electronUntouched.test.ts` stays green and is NOT weakened or repurposed; if any existing test's contract must change, it is changed openly with a stated reason, never silently. SEAM Invariant A (`window.api` attach order — D-01 puts real logic directly in that attach path for the first time since Phase 27) and Invariant B (unported channels stay non-fatal; adding handlers must never turn a warning into a crash) are both preserved. `cargo check` in `src-tauri/` stays green. *(hard constraint + SEAM Invariants A/B)*


Phase 34.2 (Tauri IPC re-plumb slice 5 — game details, settings and overrides). Minted 2026-07-25 during `/gsd-plan-phase 34.2` from 34.2-CONTEXT.md D-01..D-11 + 34.2-RESEARCH.md "Proposed Requirement Decomposition" candidate table (ROADMAP read `TBD — mint at /gsd-plan-phase 34.2`). The slice is **26 channels, no reassignments** (D-08) — unlike 34.1's D-14, `.planning/IPC-PORT-INVENTORY.md` needs no edit. **The channels are the easy part; the phase is two startup wirings the headless sidecar never runs** (REQ-34.2-02, REQ-34.2-07), both of which are already-shipped defects silently degrading Phases 30/32 today.

- [x] **REQ-34.2-01**: The nine runner-generic dispatch channels port RUNNER-GENERIC, all six managers, verbatim. `getGameInfo` (`main.ts:806`, incl. the Legendary `hasGame` fastpath, the empty-object→`null` conversion, and the `attachOverrides()` call), `getExtraInfo` (`main.ts:831`), `getGameSettings` (`main.ts:842`, incl. its own `try/catch`→`logError`→`return null` shape, which `requestGameSettings` does NOT have), `isGameAvailable` (`main.ts:801`), `kill` (`main.ts:1299` — `callAbortController(appName)` then `.stop()`, both halves verified real, not stubs: `utils/aborthandler/aborthandler.ts:12-30` and `storeManagers/steam/games.ts:1811`), `repair` (`main.ts:1128`), `changeInstallPath` (`main.ts:1304`), `getLaunchOptions` (`main.ts:1320`, incl. the synthesized `i18next.t('launch.default', …, {ns:'gamepage'})` default option), and `changeGameVersionPinnedStatus` (`main.ts:1611`, **send**-kind) are all `libraryManagerMap[runner]` dispatches ported without a runner guard. No narrowing: Phase 32 D-02 already established `storeManagers/index.ts` force-constructs all six managers in the sidecar regardless, so a guard would be a deliberate divergence from Electron with zero import-graph saving, deleted again at cutover (Phase 31 D-01's Steam-narrow precedent explicitly does NOT transfer). **MANDATORY declaration rider in `34.2-PORTED-CHANNELS.md`:** for Steam titles FOUR of these are inert *in Electron already* — `SteamGame.repair()` logs `"SteamGame.repair not implemented until Phase 2"` (`storeManagers/steam/games.ts:1610`), and `SteamLibrary.changeGameInstallPath` (`library.ts:790`), `changeVersionPinnedStatus` (`library.ts:797`) and `getLaunchOptions` (`library.ts:907`) are underscore-arg stubs (the fourth, `changeGameInstallPath`, was found during planning and is additional to CONTEXT.md D-01's three). Porting them faithfully is correct (Pitfall 4); letting a later reader parse "ported" as "repair works for Steam games" is not. *(D-01)*
- [x] **REQ-34.2-02**: i18next is INITIALIZED in the sidecar bootstrap. `i18next.use(Backend).init({...})` exists in exactly one place in the whole backend — `main.ts:460`, inside `app.whenReady()` — so under the headless sidecar the shared singleton has never been initialized. Verified empirically against the installed i18next 22.5.1: `i18next.t('notify.error.reparing', 'Error Repairing')` on the uninitialized singleton returns **`undefined`** — it does not throw, and the inline English default does NOT rescue it. `src/backend/sidecar/bootstrap.ts`'s `init()` gains an idempotent-guarded i18next init mirroring `main.ts:460-472`'s option object exactly (`backend.addPath`/`loadPath` under `join(publicDir,'locales','{{lng}}','{{ns}}[.json]')`, `returnEmptyString:false`, `returnNull:false`, `fallbackLng:'en'`, `lng` from `GlobalConfig.get().getSettings().language`, `supportedLngs: supportedLanguages`), placed AFTER `initLogger()` (so `GlobalConfig`'s first-ever read in the process can log safely) and BEFORE the `READY_SENTINEL` write. Blast radius fixed: 103 `t()` call sites across 16 backend files, of which `downloadqueue.ts`, `utils.ts`, `storeManagers/steam/library.ts`, `steam/depotErrors.ts` and `storeManagerCommon/games.ts` are ALREADY in the sidecar's import graph and have been emitting `undefined` user-facing strings since Phase 30. Phase 34.1's CR-01 found the `changeLanguage` half of this bug (which throws loudly); this is the `t()` half. **At least one test MUST exercise the REAL, uninitialized-then-initialized i18next singleton** — a suite that `jest.mock('i18next', …)` reproduces exactly 34.1 CR-01's blind spot and proves nothing. Locale-file resolution must be verified against the sidecar's own `publicDir` (`constants/paths.ts:73`, `app.getAppPath()`-anchored, which is `process.cwd()` under `electronStub`) and a missing locales directory must log loudly rather than degrade silently — the `publicdir-getapppath-chunking` / bundled-asset family of gotcha, whose packaged-build half is a named deferred-UAT item. *(D-02)*
- [x] **REQ-34.2-03**: The 19 `main.ts` handler bodies are EXTRACTED into shared Electron-free modules, not re-implemented. Carries Phase 34.1 D-07/D-08 forward unchanged: bodies move verbatim into new modules under `src/backend/gamedetails/` (following `src/backend/appshell/{language,releases,themes}.ts`'s shape and header style — note the directory is lowercase), `main.ts` keeps every `addHandler`/`addListener` line as a one-line delegation so its registration site stays auditable in one place and its diff stays mechanical, and the new curated sidecar module registers the SAME exported functions. One source of truth; no second implementation that can drift between builds. No file under `src/backend/gamedetails/` may import `electron`, `backend/ipc`, `../ipc`, `../launcher`, or `main_window` DIRECTLY — proven by a comment-stripped source gate (`gameDetailsImportGate.test.ts`), not by inspection. Transitive reach to `electron` DOES exist (e.g. `dispatch.ts` → `../dialog/dialog` → `electron`) and is EXPECTED, rescued at runtime by `electronStub`'s `Module._load` interception; the measured, enforced set of electron-importing modules reachable from this slice's entry points is committed in `electronReachLedger.test.ts`. *(Corrected 2026-07-26, plan 34.2-11: the original wording here — and two module docstrings — asserted a transitive electron-freedom property the code does not have, found by verification gap #3 / code-review finding WR-02. The depth-1 gate can only prove direct-import freedom; it was never able to see transitive edges. This sentence now states the property actually held and enforced.)* *(D-03)*
- [x] **REQ-34.2-04**: For the five feature modules the sidecar imports the UNDERLYING module, NEVER the `ipc_handler.ts`. Four need zero extraction — verified clean exports: `anticheat/utils.ts:92` → `gameAnticheatInfo`/`downloadAntiCheatData`; `wiki_game_info/wiki_game_info.ts:20` → `getWikiGameInfo`; `recent_games/recent_games.ts:50` → `removeRecentGame` (imported alone, not its `getRecentGames`/`addRecentGame`/`maxRecentGames` siblings from the same `export {}` statement); `storeSearch/cheapshark.ts:121/157/182` → `getStoreMap`/`searchGames`/`getGameDeals`. Phase 34.3 (Tauri IPC re-plumb slice 6 — shell, files, logs and diagnostics). Minted 2026-07-26 during `/gsd-plan-phase 34.3` from 34.3-CONTEXT.md D-01..D-14 + 34.3-RESEARCH.md "Proposed Requirement Decomposition" candidate table (ROADMAP read `TBD — mint at /gsd-plan-phase 34.3`). The slice is **29 channels, not 30** — the ROADMAP goal line's "30" is stale prose; `IPC-PORT-INVENTORY.md` §"Phase 34.3 — Slice 6" and 34.3-CONTEXT.md's domain boundary both say 29, confirmed by a manual count of the inventory list. **REQ-34.3-06 records a research finding that REVERSES a locked decision:** 34.3-CONTEXT.md D-05 proposed a Rust orphan-sidecar teardown fix conditional on empirical verification; research resolved (HIGH confidence, direct read of tauri 2.11.5 as pinned in `Cargo.lock`) that `AppHandle::restart()` **does** fire `RunEvent::Exit` for this codebase's worker-thread dispatch pattern, so the fix is DROPPED and the verification recorded instead. D-06 (the JS-side `relaunch(); quit()` ordering race) is a separate defect and remains in scope as REQ-34.3-07.

Two new channel-name constants land in `src/common/types/sidecarTransport.ts`. **NO `clipboard-manager:*` permission is added to `src-tauri/capabilities/default.json`** — that file's own stated principle is that Tauri v2 capabilities gate WEBVIEW→Rust IPC only and are not consulted for Rust-side plugin calls (the same reasoning that made Phase 33 WR-03 refuse `dialog:allow-open`), and the capability is scoped `"windows": ["main"]`, the window that also renders untrusted remote content — so granting clipboard *read* would hand a credential-exfiltration primitive to that content. **Research verified (HIGH confidence, direct read of the plugin's `lib.rs` at v2.3.2) that clipboard-manager contains NO `.js_init_script(...)`**, unlike `tauri-plugin-notification` whose auto-injected init IIFE crashed GameLib's startup until `notification:allow-is-permission-granted` was granted — so the zero-grant stance needs no exception. Channels: `clipboardWriteText` (`main.ts:1427`), `clipboardReadText` (L1425), `copySystemInfoToClipboard` (`utils/ipc_handler.ts:23`, via `getSystemInfo`/`formatSystemInfo` imported directly from `utils/systeminfo` per D-14). *(D-01, D-02)*

No user-visible failure toast (that would be behavior Electron does not have). Because `clipboardReadText` is typed **sync** `(): string` (`common/types/ipc.ts:417`) but registered with `addHandler`, the sidecar registration `await`s `requestRustInvoke` **itself** and never touches the stub; `electronStub.clipboard.readText` stays in place as a **documented, deliberately-dead** no-op (deleting it would make a future hook-rescued caller hit a TypeError instead of a degraded-but-safe return; making it async would make the stub lie about the Electron API it impersonates). The existing logged-no-op test at `dialogStub.test.ts:405-421` is updated **deliberately**, not deleted silently. The Humble key-copy silent-failure risk is recorded in `34.3-PORTED-CHANNELS.md` as a known accepted risk. *(D-03, D-04)*

Zero new store plumbing. *(domain scope, Claude's Discretion — dialog/push note)*
- [x] **REQ-34.3-06**: `resetHeroic`'s relaunch-teardown question is resolved EMPIRICALLY and the proposed Rust fix is DROPPED. `resetHeroic` (`main.ts:813`, body `utils.ts:466-478`) ports at parity: it deletes `configPath` and `gamesConfigPath` (`constants/paths.ts:49-50`) then, after a 1s `setTimeout`, calls `app.relaunch()` + `app.quit()`. **Research verified (HIGH confidence, direct source read of tauri 2.11.5, the version `Cargo.lock` actually pins) that `AppHandle::restart()`'s "skip the events" branch applies only when called on the MAIN thread; `dispatch_rust_channel` always runs on a `thread::spawn`'d worker (`main.rs:748-873`), so `app_relaunch` always takes the branch that requests exit through the real event loop — and the existing `RunEvent::Exit` handler (`main.rs:1042`, Phase 34 WR-03's `shutdown_child()`) runs BEFORE the process re-execs.** Therefore **no `state.shutdown_child()` call is added to the `app_relaunch` arm** and no orphan-sidecar fix is written. The verification is recorded as a code comment above the `app_relaunch` arm citing this research, and REQ-34.3-11's packaged-build live-gate item 4 is the proof artifact — a `#[cfg(test)]` unit test structurally cannot drive a live winit/tao event loop. The plan MUST NOT present the orphan as an established defect. Scope note: `resetHeroic` is **narrower than its UI copy claims** — it deletes `config.json` + `GamesConfig/` only; the Steam token + library store, all 10 Humble stores, the download manager, Wine and wiki/crossover caches all survive as siblings in `appFolder`. *(D-05, resolved: no-fix)*
- [x] **REQ-34.3-07**: The `relaunch(); quit()` ordering race is resolved INSIDE `electronStub`, not in shared backend code. In real Electron `app.relaunch()` only *registers* a restart and `app.quit()` performs it; under Tauri `app.restart()` alone does everything, and the stub forwards both as **separate fire-and-forget frames on spawned worker threads**, so `app_exit` can be processed first and the app quits instead of restarting, nondeterministically. Fix: a module-scope "relaunch in flight" flag set by `electronStub.app.relaunch()` (`electronStub.ts:180`), after which `app.quit()` (L164) and `app.exit()` become logged no-ops. **`resetHeroic`'s body in `utils.ts` stays byte-identical for both builds — NO `isTauri()` branch in shared backend code**, so Phase 35 has one less conditional to unwind; the whole divergence is contained in the stub where every other Electron-shape impedance mismatch already lives. `dispatch_rust_channel` is NOT serialized (that would touch the hot path for every `rustInvoke`). Covered by a new describe block in `sidecar/__tests__/lifecycleStub.test.ts`. This is independent of REQ-34.3-06's finding — the JS-side sequencing race and the Rust-side teardown are separate defects. *(D-06)*

Following the precedent 34.2 plan 22 set for `timeout_for()`: real `#[cfg(test)]` tests for the two clipboard arms, run manually (`cd src-tauri && cargo test`), with the **hand-verified RED proof recorded in the SUMMARY**, and their continued existence pinned by a jest source gate modelled on `src/backend/__tests__/longRunningChannels.test.ts`. Adding a cargo step to CI is explicitly **deferred** (highest-value infrastructure fix available, and this slice is its natural forcing function, but it is CI work riding inside a port slice). *(D-07)*

So it ports faithfully, `34.3-PORTED-CHANNELS.md` records per-row that "ported" does **not** mean "delete works", and the UI-lies-to-the-user bug is filed as a separate todo. **`logError` MUST NOT be registered a second time** (ported by 34.2 gap plan 34.2-16), and `logger/ipc_handler.ts` MUST NOT be side-effect-imported (it also registers the already-ported `getLogContent` at L18) — import `logger/uploader.ts` and `logger/paths.ts` directly (D-14). **Log redaction is OUT OF SCOPE (D-09):** `uploadLogFile` sends up to 10 MiB of unredacted log content to a public dpaste with a 2-day expiry, and `src/backend/logger/` has no redaction anywhere; GameLib adds Steam refresh tokens and revealed Humble key values upstream Heroic never had. The threat surface is identical in both builds, predates this slice, and the upload sits behind a confirm dialog. **The user explicitly declined even a bounded audit of whether a token or key value can actually reach a log line — file the security todo, perform no audit, and do not re-open the question during planning or execution.** *(D-08, D-09)*

- [x] **REQ-34.3-11**: A NARROW, NAMED, BLOCKING live gate is a phase-closing condition. This slice **deliberately breaks** the Phase 30 D-04 → 34.1 D-15 → 34.2 D-11 unit-proven-only precedent, and the reason is specific rather than inherited: 34.2 could honestly claim "data in / data out with assertable return shapes", but most of this slice's channels' *entire purpose* is an observable OS side effect — jest can only assert `requestRustInvoke` was called with a URL, never that a browser opened or Finder revealed anything — and it also ships two Rust arms CI never compiles plus a destructive channel whose failure mode is only visible in the process table. This is the gate 34.2 deferred as its "highest-value optional gate". **~5 named items, NOT a 29-channel UAT** (the 12 link openers share one code path, so one proves the path): (1) one opener actually opens a browser; (2) `showItemInFolder` actually reveals in Finder; (3) clipboard write pastes into a text field and `clipboardReadText` round-trips; (4) `resetHeroic` leaves **exactly ONE** sidecar process with `config.json` still gone; (5) `uploadLogFile` returns a real dpaste URL. **Build split (D-12):** items 1/2/3/5 run under `tauri:dev`; **item 4 MUST be verified on a PACKAGED build**, because `main.rs` has two sidecar spawn paths (`spawn_sidecar_dev` L668 / `spawn_sidecar_packaged` L698, selected by `use_dev_sidecar` L660) and `app.restart()` re-execs a different binary in each — the orphan question is specifically about which child gets killed. Sidecar output is read from `~/Library/Logs/GameLib/gamelib.log`, **not** the `tauri:dev` terminal. **Gate failure is BLOCKING (D-13):** findings become a gap cycle inside 34.3, exactly as Phase 33's D-13 gate surfaced and fixed three latent Tauri-parity gaps in-phase. No advisory mode, and no pre-authorized override path (a human override remains available only as a conscious act). *(D-11, D-12, D-13)*

No `IPC-PORT-INVENTORY.md` edits are needed this slice (no channel reassignments), but the inventory's Phase 34.3 section is reconciled to 29 and the ROADMAP goal's stale "30" is corrected. *(domain scope; 34.1 D-11 / 34.2 D-13 declared-list precedent)*

Phase 34.4 (Tauri IPC re-plumb slice 7 — Steam completion and Humble). Minted 2026-07-27 during `/gsd-plan-phase 34.4` from 34.4-CONTEXT.md D-01..D-10 + 34.4-RESEARCH.md "Proposed Requirement Decomposition" candidate table (ROADMAP read `TBD — mint at /gsd-plan-phase 34.4`). The slice is **31 channels, not 38** — re-scoped during discussion by D-01/D-02/D-03, which moved 6 Humble browser-auth channels to a newly-inserted Phase 34.4.1 and reassigned `isLoggedIn` (an Epic channel) to Phase 34.5. **Research CORRECTED four factual claims in CONTEXT.md, each verified against source by the orchestrator before minting:** (1) `humbleRecordGiftLinkOpened` is `addHandler`/`handle` (`humble/ipc_handler.ts:72`), **not** a `send` channel — only `logoutSteam` (`main.ts:939`) and `humbleDisconnect` (`ipc_handler.ts:121`) are `addListener`; (2) `getPrivateBranchPassword`/`setPrivateBranchPassword` are **GOG** channels (`main.ts:1510-1515` → `libraryManagerMap['gog']`), not Steam, so REQ-34.4-06 routes them through the existing `libraryManagerMap` pattern rather than any Steam module; (3) the electron-reach ledger needs **three** new baseline entries, not two — `humble/expirationAlerts.ts` (`import { Notification } from 'electron'`) is reached transitively via `humble/library.ts`; (4) `IPC-PORT-INVENTORY.md` and Phase 34.4.1's ROADMAP entry were **already edited correctly**, so D-01/D-03/D-07 are verify-intact obligations (REQ-34.4-16), not do-the-edit ones. Research also surfaced a finding CONTEXT.md did not anticipate: `electronStub.app.isPackaged` is hardcoded `false`, so porting `humbleRunValidation`'s `!app.isPackaged` guard verbatim would register a dev-only channel in every Tauri build (REQ-34.4-08).

- [x] **REQ-34.4-01**: The credential/SteamGuard login trio ports at parity. `steamStartCredentials` (`main.ts:900`), `steamSubmitGuard` (L903) and `steamPollCredential` (L899) — all `handle` — delegate to `SteamUser.startCredentialLogin` (`storeManagers/steam/user.ts:545`), `submitGuard` and `pollCredentialLogin` (L434), preserving the guard_required/poll contract at `user.ts:49-51` that the frontend depends on. `steam/user.ts` **imports zero Electron**, so this adds no reach-ledger edge and needs no stub work. The refresh token is written through the Phase 28 `keyringTokenStore` seam exactly as the already-ported QR flow does (ASVS V2). `steamAuthFlowRegistration.ts` is the direct template — the QR half of this identical flow — including its **load-bearing per-file `import '../storeManagers'`-first circular-dependency fix (L28-43), which is per-file, not once-per-bundle**, so any new registration module needs it too. **That file's L19-25 docstring, which currently asserts these three channels plus `getSteamUserInfo`/`logoutSteam` are "deliberately NOT registered" per Phase 30 D-02, becomes FALSE this slice and MUST be updated** — a stale "deliberately does NOT register" comment is precisely the kind of false invariant a future slice reads and trusts. This closes SEAM.md deferred item 5. *(domain scope, Claude's Discretion — registration shape)*
- [x] **REQ-34.4-02**: The session/identity trio ports, with `logoutSteam` registered as a `send`. `getSteamUserInfo` (`main.ts:918`) and `getSteamSyncedAt` (L922) are `handle`; **`logoutSteam` (L939) is `addListener` — a `send`, and it is the G-30-01 channel.** Its failure mode (`sidecar-send-channels-fail-silently`) produces **no reject, no timeout and no console line**, so a kind mismatch fails 100% silently and jest structurally cannot catch it — which is why REQ-34.4-13's live gate item 2 exists and why unit coverage alone is refused for this one channel. Every registration's kind is cross-checked against `main.ts`'s own `addHandler`/`addListener` call for that exact channel name, and the test asserts the kind rather than assuming it. `SteamUser.logout` (`user.ts:249`) clears the stored credential — the real security boundary (ASVS V3). *(domain scope, Claude's Discretion — send/handle cross-check)*

Bottle *status* truthfulness is live-gated as REQ-34.4-13 item 5. *(domain scope, Claude's Discretion — inline-body note)*

- [x] **REQ-34.4-07**: The 14 clean Humble channels port via curated imports, with every registration kind asserted. `humbleSync`, `humbleGetKeys`, `humbleGetSyncState`, `humbleGetUserInfo`, `humbleGetRevealedKeyValue`, `humbleMarkRedeemed`, `humbleUndoRedeemed`, `humbleGetClaimAnnotations`, `humbleGetGiftedAt`, `humbleSetOwnershipOverride`, `humbleClearOwnershipOverride`, `humbleGetOwnershipOverrides`, `humbleRecordGiftLinkOpened`, `humbleCheckHealth` — **all 14 are `handle`.** **`humbleRecordGiftLinkOpened` is `addHandler` (`humble/ipc_handler.ts:72`, confirmed by `common/types/ipc.ts:350`'s `Promise<void>` typing and `preload/api/humble.ts:31`'s `makeHandlerInvoker`) — CONTEXT.md's Discretion section wrongly listed it among the three `send` channels, and registering it as a listener would reproduce the exact G-30-01 silent-drop defect class this slice's gate exists to catch.** These are store reads or axios GETs through `humbleRequest` (`humble/adapter.ts:163`), which sets an explicit `Cookie: _simpleauth_sess=…` header from the **stored** value (L126) — which is precisely why they port with no seam at all. **The curated-import rule is at its sharpest here (30 D-08 → 34.1 D-09 → 34.2 D-04 → 34.3 D-14): `humble/ipc_handler.ts` MUST NOT be side-effect-imported**, because it also registers the 5 login channels plus `humbleRevealKey` that Phase 34.4.1 owns, and would drag `backend/ipc` in. Import `humble/user.ts`, `humble/library.ts` and `humble/validation.ts` directly. All 9 Humble stores are already registered (`storeRegistration.ts:88-97`) — zero new store plumbing. The existing `humble/__tests__/` suite already covers these channels' **logic**, so the new tests are about **registration and transport**, not logic. *(D-02 clean classification + research correction)*
- [x] **REQ-34.4-08**: `humbleRunValidation` ports dev-gated, and the `isPackaged` divergence is resolved or explicitly declared — never silently shipped. `main.ts:970` registers this dev-only validation channel behind `!app.isPackaged`. **Research found that `electronStub.app.isPackaged` is hardcoded `false`, so porting the guard verbatim would register the channel in EVERY Tauri build, packaged or not.** The plan must either resolve it (find or add a genuine packaged-vs-dev signal for the sidecar) or record the divergence explicitly as a declared row in `34.4-PORTED-CHANNELS.md` with its reasoning. What is refused is porting it verbatim and saying nothing — the minor elevation-of-privilege shape of a dev-only channel reachable in a shipped build is exactly the sort of thing that becomes invisible debt. `humbleRunValidation` imports `humble/validation.ts` directly, never `ipc_handler.ts`. *(D-02 + research finding, Open Question 1)*
- [x] **REQ-34.4-09**: `humbleDisconnect` ports as a **precisely declared partial**. It is `addListener` (`humble/ipc_handler.ts:121`) — a `send`, confirmed. `humble/user.ts`'s `disconnect` (~L577-600) calls `session.fromPartition(HUMBLE_LOGIN_PARTITION)` and clears storage; under Tauri that resolves to `electronStub.session`'s logged no-op (Phase 29 D-09). **But the stored encrypted cookie IS deleted — that is the real sign-out and the actual security boundary (ASVS V3), and it must be proven to run independently of, and not be aborted by, the no-op'd partition call** (`user.ts:588`'s comment requires exactly this). Under Tauri that partition never held anything, so surfacing a "your browser session may survive" warning is **refused as actively false** — 34.3 D-01's rider explicitly warned against inflating a precautionary no-op into a shipped defect. `34.4-PORTED-CHANNELS.md` declares per-row that "ported" means the app-side session is cleared, **plus the handoff note that once Phase 34.4.1 creates a real browser context, `humbleDisconnect` MUST be revisited to clear it too** — otherwise 34.4.1 silently inherits a half-finished sign-out. Deferring the channel wholesale is refused: its real job works in the sidecar, and deferring it would leave a Tauri user unable to sign out of Humble at all. *(D-05)*

The Steam half adds **zero** edges — `steam/user.ts`, `steam/bottle.ts` and `steam/clientSetup.ts` all import zero Electron, confirmed by measurement. The Humble half adds `humble/user.ts` (`import { safeStorage, session } from 'electron'`, L1), `humble/adapter.ts` (`import { net } from 'electron'`, L2), `humble/expirationAlerts.ts` (`import { Notification } from 'electron'`, reached transitively through `humble/library.ts:22`) — **and a FOURTH neither this requirement's own text nor RESEARCH.md predicted: `humble/userAgent.ts` (`import { app } from 'electron'`, L1), reached two-hop via `humble/user.ts:16` and independently via `humble/library.ts:12 -> ./adapter -> ./userAgent`.** Both `ENTRY_POINTS` (L44-56) and `BASELINE_ELECTRON_REACHING_MODULES` (L73+) are extended, and the baseline was **regenerated by running the tooling** (a temporary, removed `console.log` measurement statement — before: 30 modules/visitedFiles 202; after: 34 modules/visitedFiles 217), **NOT transcribed from planning-time prose** — per the ledger's own instruction, the measurement disagreement was resolved in the measurement's favor. Growth-only/subset semantics and the anti-vacuity tests are preserved and extended; the reachability floor raised 150→200 (never lowered). The committed baseline **is** the Phase 35 cutover work-list. Hand RED-proofed (removed `userAgent.ts`, growth-tripwire failed naming exactly that module, restored). See `34.4-08-SUMMARY.md`. *(34.3 D-10, repeated + research correction + execution-time discovery)*
- [x] **REQ-34.4-11**: `electronStub.net.request` is hardened to fail fast and legibly. `electronStub.ts:592` returns `{ on, end, write, setHeader }` where `on()` never fires — a silent dead end added in Phase 33 purely so `net.isOnline` could exist. **Accurate failure mode:** `humblePostRequest` (`humble/adapter.ts:264-336`) arms its own `REQUEST_TIMEOUT_MS` timer (L277-287), so this **rejects after the timeout — it does not hang forever** — but it rejects with `"Humble reveal request timed out"`, which reads as a Humble outage rather than a missing seam and would send Phase 34.4.1's owner straight back into Cloudflare territory. Fix: emit an `'error'` event **asynchronously** naming the missing seam, so `humblePostRequest`'s existing `request.on('error')` handler fires, clears its timeout, and rejects immediately with an accurate cause. **It MUST NOT reject or throw synchronously** — `sidecar-dialog-reject-crashes`: an unguarded fire-and-forget rejection hard-crashes the sidecar, and `sidecarRejectionGuard.test.ts` must stay green. Deleting `net.request` entirely is refused (an unguarded TypeError in fire-and-forget code is how that same incident happened). This is `electronStub.session`'s own stated rationale — "fails loudly with a clear log line instead of an opaque TypeError" — applied to its neighbour. The test proves the error actually reaches `humblePostRequest`'s handler, not merely that the stub emits. *(D-06)*
- [x] **REQ-34.4-12**: The silently-blank Humble login screen is replaced with an honest "not available on this build" panel. 34.1 D-12 made `getWebviewPreloadPath` return a declared-empty `''` on the sidecar, and `WebView/index.tsx:445-447` returns `<></>` when there is no preload path — so **a Tauri user who opens Humble login today gets a blank screen with no error, no log line and no explanation.** A short explanatory panel is added on the `isTauri() && !webviewPreloadPath` branch: the project's own "logged, never silent" discipline (31 D-03/D-04, 34.1 D-13) applied at the one place a user actually sees the gap, in the same slice that formally defers the fix. **Rider: it must not change Electron's behavior at all** — Electron always has a preload path, so the branch is structurally unreachable there, and that must be **proven by the test, not asserted in prose**. `electronUntouched.test.ts` is not weakened silently to accommodate the frontend edit. If the panel offers any copy affordance it uses `window.api.clipboard{Write,Read}Text`, never `navigator.clipboard` (`navigator-clipboard-noops-under-tauri`). Leaving the blank screen and merely declaring it is refused — the exact failure shape 34.3 D-01 called out for clipboard, made worse because Tauri is not yet the shipped build so nobody would catch it. Hiding the login entry point under Tauri is also refused (it hides a shipped feature behind a build check Phase 35 must unwind, and makes the gap invisible during 34.4.1's own testing). Wording and placement — small component or inline branch — are a planner call. *(D-04)*
- [x] **REQ-34.4-13**: A narrow, named, **BLOCKING 5-item live gate** is a phase-closing condition, scoped to reversible items. This carries 34.3 D-11/D-13 rather than reverting to the 34.1 D-15 / 34.2 D-11 unit-only precedent, and **the deciding evidence is `logoutSteam`**: the G-30-01 channel, a `send` whose failure mode produces no reject, no timeout and no console line — structurally invisible to assertions, and already a known past defect. The "these channels have assertable return shapes" argument is true for 14 of the 15 Steam channels and **false for the one with a known past defect**. The five items: (1) credential + SteamGuard login reaches a signed-in, populated library; (2) **`logoutSteam` actually signs out** — the send-kind check jest cannot make; (3) `getSteamUserInfo`/`getSteamSyncedAt` return real data after that login; (4) `humbleSync` + `humbleGetKeys` return the real key list; (5) `steamBottleStatus`/`isSteamBottleProvisioned` report truthfully on macOS. **Item 4 carries a NUMBERED PRECONDITION (D-09), written into the gate document rather than left implicit — an unstated precondition is exactly how a gate gets skipped and becomes UAT debt (Phases 21/30/34.2):** there is no Tauri path to create a Humble session until 34.4.1 lands, so (a) log in to Humble under `npm start` FIRST, (b) quit, (c) relaunch under `pnpm tauri:dev`, (d) sync and read keys — both builds share the same `appFolder` and encrypted `configStore` entry, so the cookie carries across. **All five items run under `tauri:dev`; there is deliberately NO packaged-build item, and the WHY is recorded IN the gate document so the omission reads as a decision rather than an oversight (D-10)** — unlike 34.3 D-12, nothing here depends on the dev-vs-packaged sidecar spawn path (these are login, sign-out, data reads and a status check, all build-independent), and the keyring path that does differ when signed is already exercised by the ported QR login. Sidecar output is read from `~/Library/Logs/GameLib/gamelib.log`, **not** the `tauri:dev` terminal. **Gate failure is BLOCKING**: findings become a gap cycle inside Phase 34.4, exactly as Phase 33's D-13 gate surfaced and fixed three latent Tauri-parity gaps in-phase. No advisory mode and no pre-authorized override path. *(D-08, D-09, D-10)*

Phase 34.4.1 (Tauri embedded-browser login seam). Minted 2026-07-27 during `/gsd-plan-phase 34.4.1` from 34.4.1-CONTEXT.md D-01..D-11 + 34.4.1-RESEARCH.md "Proposed Requirement Decomposition" candidate table (ROADMAP read `TBD — mint at /gsd-plan-phase 34.4.1`). **CONTEXT.md D-11's hard precondition — "a throwaway spike MUST RUN BEFORE PLANNING; no plan may be written on an unverified API" — was SATISFIED before minting:** spikes **013** (child `WebviewWindow` + navigation), **014a** (Rust cookie API), **014b** (injected-JS cookie read) and **015** (jar isolation/persistence) executed 2026-07-27 and are packaged into the `spike-findings-gamelib` project skill. **The spike inverted this phase's assumed risk profile:** the Rust `Webview::cookies()` API is *sound* on macOS (returns `HttpOnly`+`Secure` values in 2–4 ms, callable from any thread), so **D-03's "Humble ships declared-degraded" bad case is now unlikely rather than probable** — but `cookies_for_url()` is a genuine trap (wry compares domains with string `==`, confirmed at `wry-0.55.1/src/wkwebview/mod.rs:1184`, silently returning 4 plausible cookies for `www.humblebundle.com` with `_simpleauth_sess` **missing**), and `document.cookie` can never see the session cookie at all because it is `HttpOnly`. **Research RESOLVED D-02 in favor of Rust-arms-driven-by-the-sidecar** — not a new architectural choice but the existing `RUST_INVOKE_CHANNELS` / `dispatch_rust_channel` / `requestRustInvoke` pattern already shipped three times (Phase 28 keyring, Phase 30 `dialog_open`, Phase 34.3 clipboard); the "webview-context-only" tension CONTEXT.md names applies only to the renderer-side `@tauri-apps/api/webviewWindow` class, not to Rust's `AppHandle`-scoped `WebviewWindowBuilder`. Research also verified **all 6 channels' registration kinds against source with ZERO divergence** from CONTEXT.md (unlike 34.4, where it caught a misclassification), found **one substantive miscite** inherited from the ROADMAP into CONTEXT.md (the `<webview>` element is at `WebView/index.tsx:488-503`, **not** `:467-480` — 34.4's `WebviewUnavailablePanel` addition shifted it ~21 lines), and surfaced three APIs the spike never tested (`eval_with_callback()`, `delete_cookie()`, `on_document_title_changed`), one of which materially shapes REQ-34.4.1-05: **`eval_with_callback` does NOT auto-await a returned Promise.**

- [x] **REQ-34.4.1-01**: The shared login mechanism ships as new Rust `dispatch_rust_channel` arms (open / close / cookies / navigate) reached from the sidecar via `requestRustInvoke`, **resolving D-02 in favor of Rust-arms-driven-by-the-sidecar**. This is not new architecture — it is the `RUST_INVOKE_CHANNELS` allowlist + `dispatch_rust_channel` (`src-tauri/src/main.rs:353`) pattern already shipped by Phase 28 (keyring), Phase 30 (`dialog_open`) and Phase 34.3 (clipboard), and it keeps Phase 29 **D-01**'s "Rust is the platform seam" true while sidestepping 34.1 **D-12**'s structural finding that the headless Node sidecar cannot call the renderer-only `WebviewWindow` constructor. **Binding constraint: the 6 ported channels keep their CURRENT signatures**, so `common/types/ipc.ts` and the frontend are unaffected by the choice. **Zero new capability grants are expected** — `core:webview:allow-create-webview-window` is already granted (34.1 D-12). This reverses the 34.2/34.4 "zero new Rust arms" rider deliberately and with a stated reason, exactly as 34.3 **D-03** did for clipboard. *(D-01, D-02 — resolved by research)*
- [x] **REQ-34.4.1-02**: `humbleStartLogin`/`humbleReconnect` port at parity, preserving `watchForLogin()`'s existing poll/throttle/revalidate state machine structurally while replacing its `session.fromPartition()` + `setUserAgent` calls (`humble/user.ts` ~L256/L260) with the new `rustInvoke` arms. **The cookie read MUST use `cookies()` + a domain-suffix match, NEVER `cookies_for_url()`** — the latter silently drops `_simpleauth_sess` for `www.humblebundle.com`. **A liveness discriminator is MANDATORY: an empty cookie array must be distinguishable from "the API did nothing."** This is `navigator-clipboard-noops-under-tauri` applied to the exact failure shape that matters here — WKWebView resolved a clipboard write *without writing* and every success path still ran; a cookie read returning `[]` would be indistinguishable from "the user has not logged in yet" and `watchForLogin`'s poll would spin silently forever. Never poll on an `UNDECIDABLE` result. **REOPENED 2026-07-30 by the live gate (F-1): the from-scratch mechanism itself PASSED (34.4 D-09 falsified), but the session it persisted was stored in PLAINTEXT.** Plan 13 closed F-1 **at the code level** (keyring-backed secret store over a compile-time slot allowlist, plus a verified write→readback→compare migration for any existing plaintext value — see `REQ-34.4.1-GAP-02`). This box stays UNCHECKED until plan 20's gate re-observes item 1 against the new store and confirms no `sessionCookie` field with `encryptionDegraded: false`. *(D-01, D-02, spike 014a; reopened by 34.4.1-LIVE-GATE.md F-1, re-closed pending 34.4.1-20)*
  **CLOSED 2026-07-31 by the THIRD live gate (`34.4.1-LIVE-GATE-RERUN-3.md`, 4/4 PASS).** Item 1 was re-observed from a full from-scratch reset against the keyring-backed store: 75-byte `config.json`, **no `sessionCookie` field**, `encryptionDegraded: false`, and plan 13's `[bootstrap] Humble secret store installed: keyring-backed (humble-session/humble-csrf slots)` line present at 21:58:28 (its absence would have failed the item outright). F-1 closed live.
- [x] **REQ-34.4.1-03**: `humbleStopLogin`/`humbleLoginNavigated` port as **`send`-kind** channels (`humble/ipc_handler.ts:126-127`, `addListener` — verified against source), with the navigation relay wired to **`on_page_load`, never `on_navigation`**. `on_navigation` fires for iframes, so a third-party ad frame on the login page could re-arm the login watch deadline indefinitely. A send-vs-handle mismatch **fails 100% silently** (`sidecar-send-channels-fail-silently` — no reject, no timeout, no console line), which is precisely how G-30-01 hid; every registration is cross-checked against `humble/ipc_handler.ts`'s own `addHandler`/`addListener` call for that exact channel name. Fire-and-forget paths **resolve a safe default and never reject** (`sidecar-dialog-reject-crashes`). *(D-01, spike 013, Discretion send-vs-handle)*
- [x] **REQ-34.4.1-04**: `humbleGetLoginUserAgent` ports at parity as the pure function it is (`humble/userAgent.ts:26`, `standardBrowserUserAgent()`) — zero browser dependency, unaffected by every other decision in this phase. It travelled to 34.4.1 only because its consumer is the deferred `<webview>`'s `useragent` attribute (34.4 D-02). **Recorded explicitly so the phase is not mis-scored as "0 of 6" in D-03's bad case.** *(Discretion — pure-function note)*
- [x] **REQ-34.4.1-05**: `humbleRevealKey` gets a **TIME-BOXED** attempt at an in-webview POST from a **hidden, on-demand** child window created at reveal time and closed after (**D-08** — nothing persists between reveals; no idle window holding an authenticated Humble session, the same orphan-session concern Phase 34 **WR-03** raised). Read `humble/adapter.ts`'s `humblePostRequest` docstring (~L229-263) IN FULL first: rounds 1–6 of `humble-reveal-key-fails` **already falsified** cookie/header fidelity as the cause and identified axios's TLS/HTTP transport fingerprint as the blocker, so **rounds 1–5 MUST NOT be re-run** and reqwest/`tauri-plugin-http` are expected to fail identically. The in-webview `fetch()` is the only structurally-new option because it is the one place a genuine WKWebView fingerprint exists. **`eval_with_callback` does NOT auto-await a returned Promise** (verified against Apple/MS docs) — the return path needs the fire-and-forget eval + navigation-intercept exfil pattern, not a naive callback. On a 403 or any other failure the channel still ports, with an honest declared-degraded failure surface. **Not phase-closing.** **REOPENED 2026-07-30 as part of the gap cycle's own discipline: item 4 DID pass live (a real key was returned, not a 403), and this row's claim is NOT withdrawn.** What changed after the gate ran is diagnostics-only: plan 17 (F-8) replaced the hardcoded `"electron-net transport"` log literal with a label derived from `getLoginWindowSeam()` at log time. This box stays UNCHECKED until plan 20 re-runs item 4 as a regression check and confirms the derived label is correct with no other behavior change — a wording fix should not be checked off on the strength of a PASS measured before the wording changed. *(D-07, D-08; reopened by 34.4.1-LIVE-GATE.md F-8, re-closed pending 34.4.1-20)*
  **CLOSED 2026-07-31 by the third live gate (item 4 PASS).** A real key value was returned (not a 403) and the derived label read `login-window seam transport` x1 with `electron-net transport` x0 — F-8's wording fix confirmed by a reveal measured AFTER the change, and against a session THIS run's items 1-3 produced rather than run 2's auto-restored one. The revealed value appears in no log and no document (T-34.4.1-39), verified rather than assumed. **D-07's Cloudflare-403 declarable-failure branch was NOT exercised this run** and still rests on run 1's evidence alone.
- [x] **REQ-34.4.1-06**: `humbleDisconnect`'s declared partial (34.4 **D-09** / `IPC-PORT-INVENTORY.md` L73-75) is **CLOSED**. Under Tauri there are no partitions, so login cookies land in the **app-wide platform cookie jar**; disconnect must delete the stored encrypted credential (already works — the real security boundary, ASVS V3) **and** clear the jar's `humblebundle.com` entries so a re-login genuinely starts fresh. **Domain-scoped, NEVER a blanket wipe** — the same jar will hold Epic/GOG/Amazon cookies once 34.5 lands, and `clear_all_browsing_data()` would silently sign the user out of storefronts they never touched. Research established there is **no one-shot "clear domain X" API**: the shape is `cookies()` + suffix filter + a loop of per-cookie `delete_cookie()`. Closing this rather than re-declaring it is the whole point — **D-08** exists to stop a partial being carried for a second phase running. **REOPENED 2026-07-30 by the live gate (F-6): disconnect cleared cookies but nothing else — localStorage/sessionStorage/IndexedDB/service-workers/caches all survived, so a re-login auto-signed straight back in.** Plans 15/16 closed F-6 **at the code level** (a new origin-scoped storage-clear capability wired into a second, independent wipe step on both the Humble and Legendary branches — see `REQ-34.4.1-GAP-03`), and plan 17 built the domain-scope census (`REQ-34.4.1-GAP-05`) that makes item 3(b) observable at all. This box stays UNCHECKED until plan 20's gate re-runs item 3 in full and records a PASS on both halves. *(D-08; reopened by 34.4.1-LIVE-GATE.md F-6, re-closed pending 34.4.1-20)*
  **CLOSED 2026-07-31 by the third live gate (item 3 PASS, both halves).** Census on a real jar: `before(total=34, matched=34, verdict=SUPPORTED_NONEMPTY) after(total=0, matched=0) deleted=34`, with the reported clear count agreeing with the independent post-removal re-read. Behavioural half: the re-login was genuinely fresh — **68 `session_expired` rejections across 6 min 17 s**, against run 2's ~3 seconds and zero poll lines. Both Keychain slots went NOT FOUND and returned on re-login. F-6 closed live, at the third attempt. **The domain-scoping property itself remains UNTESTED — see the rider on `REQ-34.4.1-GAP-05`; this closure is about deletion and session destruction, not about scope.**
- [x] **REQ-34.4.1-07**: The `!webviewPreloadPath` branch (34.4 **D-04**) is **SPLIT**. Login routes stop rendering `WebviewUnavailablePanel` and drive the new mechanism; store/wiki routes (`/store/epic|gog|amazon|zoom|steam`, `/wiki`, `store-page?store-url=`) keep the panel with copy naming the **real** gap ("in-app store browsing isn't available on this build") plus an **Open in browser** affordance using the already-ported `openExternalUrl`. In-app store/wiki browsing is **OUT OF SCOPE** (**D-05**) — it has genuinely different requirements (the `WebviewControls` chrome, `sessionStorage` last-url restore, `validStoredUrl` host validation, the adtraction redirect workaround) and this split keeps that deferral visible to users, not only to the roadmap. **34.4 D-04's rider carries unchanged: Electron's behavior must not change, and that must be PROVEN IN A TEST, not asserted.** Any copy affordance uses `window.api.clipboard{Write,Read}Text`, never `navigator.clipboard`. **Locate the JSX by reading current source — the `<webview>` element is at `WebView/index.tsx:488-503`, not the `:467` both ROADMAP.md and CONTEXT.md cite.** *(D-05, D-06)*
- [x] **REQ-34.4.1-08**: The four OAuth runners (Epic/`legendary`, GOG, Amazon/`nile`, Zoom) are **wired** to the shared mechanism's navigation observation — they capture an OAuth code out of a redirect URL, not a cookie — but their backend auth channels (`login`, `authGOG`, `authAmazon`, `authZoom`) stay **Phase 34.5's** and keep returning `UNPORTED_CHANNEL_MARKER` per SEAM Invariant B. Those four logins therefore still fail after this phase: **declared, not silently broken.** The mechanism is proven end-to-end through **Humble** instead, because Humble is the only one of the five whose backend is already ported (34.4 shipped its other 15 channels). **Navigation observation — not the cookie read — is what Phase 34.5 actually inherits**, so a plan that treats the cookie read as the critical path has the risk inverted. *(D-04)*
- [x] **REQ-34.4.1-09**: **Fail-closed window labelling.** Every window this phase creates uses a **generated** label — never `main`, never `about`, never derived from a caller-supplied URL (**T-34.1-27**) — matching **NO** capability in `src-tauri/capabilities/default.json`, and therefore receiving **zero** Tauri command access. This directly answers 34.3 **D-02**'s landmine (`"windows": ["main"]` shares grants with untrusted remote content — the capability file's own `description` names the Humble login flow explicitly). **No capability-file edit is expected and child labels must NEVER join the `windows` array**; no second capability file may be created for them. `tauriChildWindows.ts` already solves this for renderer-created windows (WR-04 labels survive renderer reload; WR-07 no hard-coded title, deliberately, as an anti-phishing measure; WR-06 float the `once()` promise) — extend that discipline to the Rust side, and extend `src/preload/__tests__/childWindows.test.ts` rather than restarting it. *(D-01, T-34.1-27, 34.3 D-02)*
- [x] **REQ-34.4.1-10**: `34.4.1-PORTED-CHANNELS.md` declares all **6** rows with port kind (`sidecar invoke` / `sidecar send`) and **honest proof level** — unit-proven, live-gate-proven, or ported-with-a-declared-caveat. SEAM.md's §3 browser-auth rows move to §1 (Incremental-Port Checklist steps 5/6). `IPC-PORT-INVENTORY.md`'s existing Phase 34.4.1 section (L77-86) and the `humbleDisconnect` partial note (L73-75) are **verified intact, not re-edited** — research confirmed both are already correct. Where an automated test proves only wiring, the artifact says so plainly: **no reader may take "wired and unit-proven" for "seen working"**, the conflation that let G-30-02 be declared fixed twice while the live build hung. **34.4 D-09's manual precondition — "there is no Tauri path to create a Humble session until 34.4.1 lands" — is struck from the record rather than left standing** once gate item 1 passes. *(D-03, D-10, SEAM checklist step 5)*
- [x] **REQ-34.4.1-11**: The additive/reversible invariant holds **BEHAVIORALLY**: `npm start` (Electron) and `pnpm tauri:dev` both work after **every** plan, with zero change to Electron's observable behavior — but as in 34.1/34.2/34.3/34.4 this phase **does** touch Electron-side files by design (`WebView/index.tsx` per REQ-34.4.1-07), so the invariant is behavioral, not textual. `electronUntouched.test.ts` and `sidecarRejectionGuard.test.ts` stay green and are **not weakened silently**; `electronReachLedger.test.ts` is extended for any new Node-side module edge (34.3 **D-10**'s standing obligation), **regenerated at execution time, never from planning-time prose** — `humble/user.ts` and `humble/adapter.ts` are already baselined by 34.4. `npx tsc --noEmit` and `cargo check` stay clean. **Two cheap sweeps are folded in as tasks (Discretion):** (a) a stale-`isTauri()`-guard sweep of the login path — **34.4's gate item 2 failed on exactly this**, a Phase 30 guard in `GlobalState.tsx` whose own comment asserted a premise a later plan had falsified, leaving a ported channel unreachable while every unit test, `tsc`, `cargo check` and the reach ledger stayed green (research confirmed that specific guard is now gone, so this is a **fresh sweep of this phase's own diff**, not a remediation); and (b) a bare-import build-regression check, which 34.4-10 caught while invisible to both `tsc` and jest but fatal to `npm start`. *(SEAM Invariants A/B, 34.3 D-10, Discretion)*
- [x] **REQ-34.4.1-12** (gate RAN 2026-07-30 per `34.4.1-LIVE-GATE.md` — **VERDICT FAIL, 2 of 4 clean** — items 2/4 PASS, item 1 PASS-WITH-DEFECT (F-1), item 3 FAIL (F-6); the gap cycle, plans 10-19, closed both blocking defects at the CODE level; this box stays UNCHECKED until plan 20's re-run records 4/4 PASS — reopened alongside REQ-34.4.1-02/-05/-06, not left as the original "gate not yet run" note once the gate had, in fact, run and failed): A **BLOCKING 4-item live gate** under `pnpm tauri:dev` is a phase-closing condition, carrying 34.3 **D-13** and 34.4 **D-08**. The items: (1) **a Humble login completed FROM SCRATCH** with no Electron-captured session — the headline deliverable, and precisely the thing 34.4 **D-09** had to declare impossible; (2) **the session survives quit-and-relaunch**, proven at the persistence layer (the present-to-absent store proof 34.4-10 established, stronger than a UI check); (3) **`humbleDisconnect` leaves no `humblebundle.com` cookie** and a re-login is genuinely fresh (**D-08**); (4) **`humbleRevealKey`'s real outcome recorded, pass or 403** (**D-07**) — reveal IS live-testable, because per Phase 14's D-30 realignment `redeemed_key_val` means **revealed, not activated**, so unlike 34.4 D-08's refused `redeemSteamKey` it is irreversible at Humble but not destructive. **Preconditions MUST be written as explicit numbered steps** — an unstated precondition is exactly how a gate gets skipped and becomes UAT debt (Phases 21/30/34.2). Sidecar output is read from `~/Library/Logs/GameLib/gamelib.log`, **not** the `tauri:dev` terminal. **Gate failure is BLOCKING**: findings become a gap cycle inside 34.4.1. No advisory mode, no pre-authorized override. *(D-09, D-10)*
  **CLOSED 2026-07-31 — the gate RAN A THIRD TIME and the VERDICT IS 4/4 PASS** (`34.4.1-LIVE-GATE-RERUN-3.md`). Run 1 FAILED 2/4, run 2 FAILED 3/4, run 3 PASSES 4/4. Item 1 PASS (from-scratch keyring-backed login; WR-07 and F-4 both closed), item 2 PASS (store byte-identical across a real relaunch, both PIDs changed, plus a 29/29 authenticated gamekey sync proving the restored session is functional rather than merely present), item 3 PASS (F-6 closed live), item 4 PASS (seam transport label holds). No item recorded FAIL; D-10's binding rule was satisfied with no advisory mode, no override and no partial-pass closure. **Three live runs were needed, and the suite was fully green for all three: 3279/3279 missed F-1 and F-6; 3387/3387 missed F-6's second defect.**
- [x] **REQ-34.4.1-13**: **macOS is LIVE-PROVEN; Linux and Windows are verified by reading the crate and DECLARED per-platform** in `34.4.1-PORTED-CHANNELS.md`, naming the specific unverified surfaces (`cookies_for_url()`'s domain-match correctness on WebKitGTK/WebView2, the UA fingerprint gap, `data_store_identifier`'s macOS-14+/iOS-17+ gating). Tauri's webview is **WKWebView** (macOS), **WebKitGTK** (Linux), **WebView2/Chromium** (Windows) — three cookie-API stories, three navigation-event stories, three TLS fingerprints — and **macOS, the primary dev platform, is furthest from the Chromium stack that currently works**. This matches every prior slice's precedent (34.3 D-12 and 34.4 D-10 were macOS-only live gates). Recording the per-platform finding makes the omission read as a **decision, not an oversight**. Test-proven safe degradation for the other two platforms is volunteered **if research found it cheap** — `navigator-clipboard-noops-under-tauri` is the live reminder that an API can resolve successfully while doing nothing. *(D-09, Discretion)*

**Gap-cycle requirements (minted 2026-07-30 during plan 34.4.1-19, from `34.4.1-LIVE-GATE.md`'s
Verdict and its post-gate D-GAP-01/D-GAP-02 decisions — not from a CONTEXT.md decision that
predates them.** Each extends an existing requirement above; none replaces it. A box is checked
here only where the gap-cycle plan's own evidence is unit/cargo-proven and does not depend on
plan 20's live-gate re-run; where the requirement's own claim IS what plan 20 must observe, it
stays unchecked alongside the existing ID it extends, per this plan's own no-upgrade-on-unit-
evidence discipline.

- [x] **REQ-34.4.1-GAP-01**: A compile-time **multi-slot keyring allowlist** replaces the
  single-slot `KEYRING_SERVICE`/`KEYRING_ACCOUNT` pair `src-tauri/src/main.rs` previously
  hardcoded. `keyring_account()` validates a sidecar-supplied slot name (`steam-refresh-token`,
  `humble-session`, `humble-csrf`) against a fixed match arm and rejects any unknown slot with
  `keyring:unknown-slot` — **T-28-03 (the sidecar must not address an arbitrary Keychain entry) is
  preserved, not reopened.** Two alternatives were considered and rejected on the record: a
  dedicated `keyring_humble_*` channel family (duplicates ~60 lines of Rust per secret, does not
  scale) and a free-form account argument taken from the sidecar's own `args` (smallest diff, but
  explicitly reopens T-28-03). Proven by plan 11's unit/cargo tests independent of any live gate —
  the Steam slot's own behavior is unchanged and tested. *(D-GAP-01; extends REQ-28-02/REQ-28-03,
  the TokenStore seam)*
- [x] **REQ-34.4.1-GAP-02**: The **Humble session is encrypted at rest under Tauri**, closing F-1.
  `SidecarHumbleSecretStore` (plan 13) binds `sessionCookie`/`csrfToken` to the
  `humble-session`/`humble-csrf` keyring slots (`REQ-34.4.1-GAP-01`), with a one-time
  write→readback→exact-match-compare migration for any existing plaintext value that leaves an
  already-signed-in user untouched on any failure of that chain. **Closed at the CODE level only**
  — proven by plan 13's unit tests (totality, slot isolation, the full migration failure matrix),
  never by a live Keychain write. This box stays UNCHECKED until plan 20's gate re-observes item 1
  and confirms the store holds no `sessionCookie` field, `encryptionDegraded: false`, and plan
  13's `[bootstrap] Humble secret store installed: keyring-backed (humble-session/humble-csrf
  slots)` log line. *(D-GAP-01, D-GAP-02; extends REQ-34.4.1-02, closes F-1)*
  **CLOSED 2026-07-31 by the third live gate (item 1 PASS).** The store was read live during item 1: 75 bytes, `encryptionDegraded: false`, `isLoggedIn: true`, `sessionCookie` and `csrfToken` both ABSENT (held in the Keychain slots `humble-session`/`humble-csrf`, confirmed present with `mdat` matching login acceptance to the second). The keyring-install log line was present, so the install did not silently degrade to the plaintext default.
- [x] **REQ-34.4.1-GAP-03**: An **origin-scoped storage-clear capability** (`humble_login_clear_storage`
  Rust arm + `LoginWindowSeam.clearStorage()`, plan 15) clears `localStorage`/`sessionStorage`/every
  IndexedDB database/every Cache Storage entry/every service-worker registration for one origin,
  scoped by same-origin-policy construction rather than a filter, with honest per-category
  `count | 'unsupported'` reporting (never a coerced `0`). Plan 16 wires it into a second,
  independent `wipeSteps` entry on both `humble/user.ts disconnect()` and the deliberate
  cross-phase edit to `storeManagers/legendary/user.ts logout()` (Phase 34.5's file — F-6's

  `seamBranchParity.test.ts`'s enforced-parity guard proven load-bearing by a temporary revert).
  Whether it actually stops a re-login from auto-signing back in has NOT been observed live** —
  this box stays UNCHECKED alongside `REQ-34.4.1-06` until plan 20's gate re-runs item 3 and
  records a PASS. *(D-GAP-02; extends REQ-34.4.1-06, closes F-6 at the code level)*
  **CLOSED 2026-07-31 by the third live gate (item 3 PASS).** The per-category storage report fired and is well-formed: `localStorage=29, sessionStorage=0, indexedDB=1, caches=0, serviceWorkers=0` — every category a number, none the literal `unsupported`, none a coerced fallback masquerading as a count. The two non-zero counts prove the reporting path is live rather than uniformly stubbed. Caveat recorded honestly: this single line cannot distinguish a genuine empty category from a coerced `0` for the three zeros individually.
- [x] **REQ-34.4.1-GAP-04**: A mechanical **seam-parity sweep + regression guard** (plan 10's
  two-axis audit of every `seam === null`/`seam !== null` branch pair, upgraded by plan 16 from a
