- [x] **REQ-34.4.1-GAP-05**: A paired before/after **cookie-jar census**, taken from inside
  `disconnect()`'s cookie-clear step against the same still-open window label the clear itself
  uses (plan 17), makes the domain-scope claim (`humbleDisconnect` clears Humble's cookies without
  touching any other origin's) observable at all — closing F-5, the original gate's finding that
  no surface could prove or disprove this. The census's own arithmetic (three independent
  equalities, each failure named separately) is unit-proven. **Whether a REAL disconnect's
  arithmetic actually comes out clean — the thing this instrument exists to show — is plan 20 item
  3(b)'s job, not this plan's.** This box stays UNCHECKED alongside `REQ-34.4.1-06` until plan 20
  reads the census line from a live disconnect and confirms the arithmetic holds. *(extends
  REQ-34.4.1-06, closes F-5)*
  **CLOSED 2026-07-31 for the census arithmetic (item 3(b) PASS), with ONE PROPERTY EXPLICITLY UNTESTED.** Three of four checks passed cleanly on a real jar: `matched-after` 0, the jar shrank by exactly `matched-before`, `deleted` equalled `matched-before`. **`survivingNonHumble` was 0, which is VACUOUS rather than failing:** `before total=34` equalled `matched=34`, so no non-Humble cookie existed for the delete to spare and the zero is arithmetically forced. **Root cause is this phase's own contract, not the operator:** precondition 6 of `34.4.1-LIVE-GATE-RERUN-3.md` STRUCK the planted non-Humble cookie as moot, reasoning that the census supplies the evidence without it. That conflates the measuring apparatus with the thing measured — a census cannot report `survivingNonHumble > 0` against a single-origin jar however correct its counting. **The domain-scoping guarantee is therefore NOT live-proven. The next cycle must UNSTRIKE precondition 6** and re-run item 3(b) against a jar holding at least one non-Humble cookie.
- [x] **REQ-34.4.1-GAP-06**: **SteamGridDB's API key (F-1b) is closed by EVIDENCED DECLARATION,
  not encryption.** Plan 14 re-measured `steamgrid/secureKey.ts`'s reachability with two
  independent instruments (`electronReachLedger.test.ts`'s live traversal and a direct
  `grep -rn "steamgrid" src/backend/sidecar/`) and confirmed it remains unreachable from the
  sidecar's curated import graph — its only importer is `src/backend/main.ts`, Electron's own
  entry point. No Rust allowlist slot was added (nothing can write to it yet); a 20-line in-source
  comment on `secureKey.ts` names the evidence and the trigger condition that would make it live.
  **This is a genuinely different, weaker claim than `REQ-34.4.1-GAP-02`'s** — nothing is encrypted
  because nothing is reachable, and no gate item exercises this surface, so there is nothing for
  plan 20 to re-observe. Forward obligation: whichever future plan first ports a SteamGridDB
  channel into the sidecar MUST migrate onto the `HumbleSecretStore`-shaped seam and add its
  allowlist slot BEFORE porting the channel. *(D-GAP-01; extends REQ-34.4.1-GAP-01, closes F-1b)*

Gap cycle 2 (minted 2026-07-31 during `34.4.1-21` from `34.4.1-LIVE-GATE-RERUN.md` § Verdict +
`34.4.1-RESEARCH-GAP-CYCLE-2.md`). Same no-upgrade-on-unit-evidence discipline as GAP-01..06 above:
a box stays `[ ]` when the claim is what the THIRD blocking live gate (plan 29) must observe, and
is `[x]` only where the requirement's own claim is genuinely unit/cargo/script-provable without a
live run.

- [x] **REQ-34.4.1-GAP-07**: The disconnect **census** reads the cookie jar through a
  correctly-directed domain match. `cookie_domain_matches` is directional
  (`host.ends_with(".{domain}")` only fires when `host` is the narrower string); the census call
  site (`humble/user.ts:984`) currently passes the fixed target as `host` and each cookie's own
  domain as `domain` — the reverse of the direction the arm's suffix check requires — so it only
  ever matches cookies whose domain attribute is the bare apex string, silently undercounting
  every subdomain/leading-dot-scoped Humble cookie. Plan 22 gives the census its own
  correctly-directed arm (or an explicit `mode` argument) rather than reusing an arm whose
  direction was designed for `watchForLogin()`'s page-host-vs-cookie-domain question. This box
  stays UNCHECKED until plan 29 records a live corrected census. *(extends REQ-34.4.1-GAP-05,
  REQ-34.4.1-06; closes F-6's Defect A)*
  **CLOSED 2026-07-31 by the third live gate (item 3 PASS).** The corrected, correctly-directed census read live: `matched=34` against `total=34` — it now finds every Humble cookie in the jar. Run 2's undercounting shape (a report of 25 against a jar that shrank by 1, with all 23 Humble cookies surviving) does not reproduce. The BEFORE read's `total=34` / `verdict=SUPPORTED_NONEMPTY` also satisfies the read-path check, so the read arm is verified working rather than merely not-observed-broken.
- [x] **REQ-34.4.1-GAP-08**: The domain-scoped cookie **clear** actually removes cookies, and its
  reported count is VERIFIED by a post-removal re-read rather than computed before the operation
  runs. Source-verified root cause (`wry-0.55.1/src/wkwebview/mod.rs:1248-1267`):
  `delete_cookie()` reconstructs an `NSHTTPCookie` from scratch via
  `cookieWithProperties(_:)`, and for a pure session cookie with no explicit Max-Age/Expires (the
  common shape for `_simpleauth_sess`) the reconstructed object's Version/Expires block is skipped
  entirely — `WKHTTPCookieStore.deleteCookie(_:completionHandler:)`'s completion handler fires
  unconditionally regardless of whether anything actually matched and was removed (same shape as
  WebKit bugzilla #184938). Plan 23 replaces the reconstruct-and-delete path with
  `WKWebsiteDataStore.fetchDataRecords`/`removeData(for:)` reached through
  `WebviewWindow::with_webview()` + `objc2-web-kit`, which hands back and removes WebKit's own
  *live* record objects — no round-trip identity question. Domain-scoped by `displayName`, so
  D-08's never-a-blanket-wipe constraint still holds. This box stays UNCHECKED — live-only.
  *(extends REQ-34.4.1-06; closes F-6's Defect B, the BLOCKING finding)*
  **CLOSED 2026-07-31 by the third live gate (item 3 PASS) — the BLOCKING finding is closed.** The clear reported 34 and the independent post-removal re-read confirmed 34 (`after total=0, matched=0`). The contract's tie-breaker (if the reported count and the census disagree, the census wins and the item fails) was not needed: they agree. Corroborated outside the log — both Keychain slots reported NOT FOUND and the store collapsed from 75 bytes to `{}` — so the teardown reached all three stores, not just the jar. Defect B, the attempted-count that could never report failure, is gone.
- [x] **REQ-34.4.1-GAP-09**: The login window's presentation is live-observed on BOTH halves: the
  OS title bar shows the loaded document's own title (WR-07's positive claim — `34.4.1-20`'s gate
  found it still reads the framework default `Tauri app`, which the existing grep-based gate
  structurally cannot prove either way) and the one-shot raise/focus behaviour is observed rather
  than assumed (F-4's unobserved half). This box stays UNCHECKED — live-only. *(extends
  REQ-34.4.1-09; closes WR-07 and F-4)*
  **CLOSED 2026-07-31 by the third live gate — BOTH halves live-observed, at the third attempt.** Title half: the bar read exactly `Humble Bundle - Log In` (22 characters), an exact match to plan 24's `title change applied len=22` diagnostic, reversing run 2's stuck-at-`Tauri app` failure. Raise half (**unobserved in BOTH prior runs**): the window came to the front and took keyboard focus on creation. The requested presentation line `visible=true width=900 height=700 center=true focus_once=true persistent_pin=false` was present and **matched observed behaviour on both halves** — including that the window does not follow the app on re-activation, which is `persistent_pin=false` behaving as chosen, not a defect. WR-07 and F-4 both closed. Residual, non-blocking: a visible `Tauri app` flash between window presentation and first title application (root cause established from ordering; the `len=0` empty-title application was proposed as the cause and REFUTED).
- [x] **REQ-34.4.1-GAP-10**: A mechanical WKWebView silent-no-op sweep exists, runs, and files its
  output as a BACKLOG rather than resolving every finding inline; and `seam-parity-sweep.py`'s own
  staleness (S-07/S-10/S-11 — `categories_for_labels()` not recognizing
  `clearHumbleStorage`/`clearEpicStorage`, `secretStore.ts`'s doc comment not satisfying
  `is_axis_b_declared()`'s id+term bar) is closed. This is a testing instrument, not a live
  behaviour — proven correct by its own self-tests, same shape as `REQ-34.4.1-GAP-04`. No
  live-gate dependency. *(extends REQ-34.4.1-GAP-04, REQ-34.4.1-11)*
- [ ] **REQ-34.4.1-GAP-11**: `keyring_get` cannot silently consume the sidecar's whole 60s RPC
  budget — it returns a classified, bounded-timeout error instead of hanging until the outer
  `RUST_INVOKE_TIMEOUT_MS` fires with no diagnostic. Explicitly an OBSERVABILITY requirement, not
  a root-cause fix for F-9 (the `keyring_get`/`humble-csrf` 60s timeout finding), whose underlying
  cause is not yet established. This box stays UNCHECKED — live-only. *(extends
  REQ-34.4.1-GAP-01)*
- [x] **REQ-34.4.1-GAP-12**: The Manage Accounts (`/login`) route renders on FIRST navigation, not
  only on retry. Research falsified the original suspected cause — `App.tsx:147-150` DOES already
  register a per-route lazy boundary (eager `import()` at module eval, no `errorElement`
  anywhere), and `Login/index.tsx:118-120` renders a visible spinner — so a truly blank first-nav
  window points UPSTREAM of the lazy-loaded component itself, not at a missing Suspense boundary.
  This box stays UNCHECKED — live-observed. *(extends REQ-34.4.1-12, it blocked
  `34.4.1-20`'s item 3 gate; closes F-10)*
  **CLOSED 2026-07-31 — both conditions this box required are satisfied.** (1) The F-10 pre-check rendered Manage Accounts **first-time on a cold start**: no blank window, no retry. (2) Plan 25 recorded a **classified fix, not a non-reproduction** — the root cause was measured (`.App .content`'s `min-height: 100%` resolving against `.App`'s `1fr` grid row, which WebKit converges to a garbage 23323.0625px row) and the one-line fix was confirmed on hardware. This box's own bar — *a non-reproduction is not a closure* — is met because the closure rests on plan 25's measurement, with this run's render serving as the regression check.
- [x] **REQ-34.4.1-GAP-13**: Known WKWebView-degraded frontend capabilities are GUARDED rather
  than assumed. Adopts two rows that sat "unassigned" in `34.4.1-LIVE-GATE-RERUN.md`'s findings
  register: `queryLocalFonts` (Chromium-only, throws under WKWebView) cannot escape as an
  unhandled rejection and instead degrades to the CSS-declared default fonts; and no image source
  is wrapped in the `imagecache://` URL scheme on a shell that does not register it (Electron-only,
  ~150 guaranteed `unsupported URL` failures per library render under Tauri), with the scheme
  decision held in one named predicate (`imageCacheSchemeAvailable()` in
  `preload/tauriTransport.ts`) rather than a platform sniff repeated per consumer. **Split claim:**
  the `queryLocalFonts` half is closed by unit evidence alone — a capability check is fully
  testable by deleting/throwing the API on `globalThis`, which plan 27's
  `queryLocalFontsSafe.test.ts`-equivalent coverage does directly, including unhandled-rejection
  tracking. The artwork half's user-visible proof — that the library renders with zero
  `unsupported URL` console errors under a real Tauri build — is a LIVE observation belonging to
  plan 29's gate; unit tests here only prove no `imagecache://` URL is ever emitted when the
  scheme is unavailable, not that the resulting page is error-free end to end. This box stays
  UNCHECKED on that live-only half. *(extends REQ-34.4.1-11; adopts the `queryLocalFonts` and
  ~150 percent-encoded Steam artwork URL findings from `34.4.1-LIVE-GATE-RERUN.md`)*

Phase 34.5 (Tauri IPC re-plumb slice 8 — non-Steam runners, Wine and shortcuts). Minted 2026-07-29 during `/gsd-plan-phase 34.5` from 34.5-CONTEXT.md D-01..D-15 + 34.5-RESEARCH.md "Proposed Requirement Decomposition" candidate table (ROADMAP read `TBD — mint at /gsd-plan-phase 34.5`). The slice is **38 channels ported, not 57** — re-scoped during discussion by D-01/D-02/D-03, which **drop Zoom permanently** (3 channels — `authZoom`, `getZoomUserInfo`, `logoutZoom`) and **defer 16 to a newly-required Phase 34.6** (EOS overlay 8, SteamGridDB artwork 5, winetricks 3). **Research CORRECTED three claims in CONTEXT.md, each verified against source by the orchestrator before minting:** (1) **D-15's dialog citation is wrong** — `tools/index.ts:794` calls only `appendMessage`/`logWarning`, has no dialog of any kind, and sits inside `Winetricks.checkDependencies`, a **deferred** cluster unreachable from any of the 38; the real in-scope dialog is `showDialogBoxModalAuto` at `tools/index.ts:137` (`installOrUpdateTool`, reached via `toggleDXVK`/`toggleVKD3D`), and it is **already safe** — `electronStub.dialog` is Rust-backed and non-throwing, so D-15's "dialog reject crashes the app" landmine does not apply to this phase's surface; (2) **D-10 names only one of two `getPath('exe')` call sites** — `shortcuts/shortcuts/shortcuts.ts:227` (`generateMacOsApp`'s `run.sh` launch command) is a second, independent site with the identical failure mode, so neither the fix nor its live proof may be scoped to `nonesteamgame.ts` alone; (3) **D-12 misnames nile's CLI-credential directory** — it is `nile_config` (`nile/constants.ts:4`, wired via `NILE_CONFIG_PATH` at `nile/library.ts:486`), while `nile_store` (`nile/electronStores.ts:13`) is the *profile* `configStore` cwd, the other half of D-12's own distinction; both resolve through `pathShim`'s already-implemented `userData`, so no scope consequence. Research also **resolved a Discretion question CONTEXT.md left open**: `documents` (D-09's third missing `pathShim` name) belongs to the **saves-sync** cluster (`save_sync.ts:146`, `syncGOGSaves`), not shortcuts — so saves-sync is blocked on the wave-1 shim work too. And it surfaced a finding CONTEXT.md did not anticipate: **`runWineCommand` has never actually run for a non-Steam runner under the sidecar** (its Steam path is proven via already-ported `install`/`launch`, but no non-Steam login has ever worked there), which narrows D-14's wave-1 burden from "build it" to "prove it live once" (REQ-34.5-03/12).
  **CLOSED 2026-07-31 on BOTH halves.** The `queryLocalFonts` half was already closed by plan 27's unit evidence. The image half — the only part plan 27 could not close by unit test — is now closed live: a full Library re-render (navigated away and back, Errors filter on, via the force-opened Web Inspector) produced **ZERO** `unsupported URL` errors, against a pre-fix baseline of ~150 per single render. The two outcomes differ by two orders of magnitude, so this is not a marginal reading. No `imagecache://` URL is emitted against the unregistered scheme.

- [x] **REQ-34.5-01**: **Wave-1 seam 1 — `pathShim` extension + `exe` semantics.** `src/backend/sidecar/pathShim.ts` gains the three missing path names (`desktop`, `exe`, `documents`), verified against source as implementing only 4 (`appData`, `userData`, `temp`, `home`) and **throwing loudly** on anything else. `exe` resolves to the **Tauri shell binary** via a new `GAMELIB_SHELL_EXE` environment variable set by `src-tauri/src/main.rs` at sidecar-spawn time on **both** `spawn_sidecar_dev` and `spawn_sidecar_packaged` — **not** a `rustInvoke` round-trip (`getPath()` is synchronous) and **not** `process.execPath` (which is node, and would write the interpreter into a Steam VDF). Covers **both** `exe` call sites: `nonesteamgame.ts:258` and the previously-uncited `shortcuts.ts:227`. Unset `GAMELIB_SHELL_EXE` **throws loudly, never returns an empty string** — matching pathShim's existing convention and preventing a silently-bad launch command. Rust-side path formatting is extracted into an `AppHandle`-free pure helper with `#[cfg(test)]` tests, mirroring the existing `clipboard_text_arg`/`login_window_url_arg` pattern. *(D-07, D-09, D-10; research Correction 3)*
- [x] **REQ-34.5-02**: **Wave-1 seam 2 — host-anchor nile's redirect match, closing the inherited `T-34.4.1-44b`.** nile captures its OAuth code via a **host-free param match** inherited from the Electron original — harmless in 34.4.1 only because the receiving channel rejects; **this phase mints a real credential from it, so the anchor must land BEFORE any credential flow exists.** `matchOAuthRedirect`'s `nile` case (`src/backend/sidecar/oauthLoginCapture.ts`) is anchored on `www.amazon.com`. Zoom's half of the obligation is **void** (D-02 drops it). Research verified `legendary` and `gog` are **already** host-anchored, so only nile carries the defect. The `www.amazon.com` value is **MEDIUM confidence** — verified against upstream `imLinguin/nile`'s `openid.return_to`, **not** against GameLib's vendored binary — so it MUST be confirmed against one real captured redirect during the REQ-34.5-12 live gate before shipping. *(D-07, D-11; research Assumption A1)*
- [x] **REQ-34.5-03**: **Wave-1 seam 3 — `runWineCommand` execution seam + the 9 Wine channels.** `runWineCommand` is a seam, not a cluster member: its consumers span launcher and tools beyond the 9. All 9 Wine channels (`getAlternativeWine`, `installWineVersion`, `refreshWineVersionInfo`, `removeWineVersion`, `runWineCommand`, `toggleDXVK`, `toggleDXVKNVAPI`, `toggleVKD3D`, `wine.isValidVersion`) port **as-is, trusting the existing platform guards** — verified unchanged and correct at `tools/index.ts:73` (`tool.os !== process.platform`), `:219` (`-DXMT` DirectX→Metal builds), `:228` (macOS permits `dxvk` only; **VKD3D already excluded**), `:245` (`dxvk-macOS`), `:642` (`isMac ? macEnvs : linuxEnvs`), `:807`/`:843` (`if (!isLinux)`). The sidecar is a real node process on the same OS, so `process.platform` behaves identically; re-deciding platform scope would mean re-implementing correct upstream behavior. **The seam is thin pass-through plumbing (pure Node `child_process`, zero Electron dependency) that already works transitively for Steam — the real burden is proving it live once for a NON-Steam runner, a combination that has never run under the sidecar.** *(D-07, D-13, D-14; research Pitfall 2)*
- [x] **REQ-34.5-04**: The **11 auth + sign-out channels** are ported — `login`, `getUserInfo`, `isLoggedIn`, `getEpicGamesStatus`, `logoutLegendary` (Epic); `authGOG`, `logoutGOG` (GOG); `authAmazon`, `getAmazonLoginData`, `getAmazonUserInfo`, `logoutAmazon` (Amazon). They consume 34.4.1-09's already-wired `oauthCaptureLogin` navigation-observation path — **the runners are wired, not greenfield**, and the UI already shows a declared-blocked surface naming each runner's unported channel, which this requirement converts to working. **Amazon depends on REQ-34.5-02 landing first**; Epic/GOG have no equivalent precondition. `isLoggedIn` arrives here by reassignment from 34.4 (D-03). **Registration-kind cross-check is mandatory: `logoutGOG` is `addListener`/send-kind** — a mis-registered send channel fails *silently* under the sidecar (no reject, no timeout, no log line), which for a sign-out is a repudiation risk, not a transport nit. *(D-01, D-04, 34.4 D-03)*
- [x] **REQ-34.5-05**: The **7 shortcuts + add-to-Steam channels** are ported — `addShortcut`, `removeShortcut`, `shortcutsExists`, `processShortcut`, `addToSteam`, `removeFromSteam`, `isAddedToSteam`. **Hard-blocked on REQ-34.5-01**: this cluster **throws the moment it is ported** until `pathShim` implements `desktop` and `exe` (D-09's own framing — wave-1 work, not a mid-execution discovery). `addToSteam` carries the `exe`-in-VDF risk directly (`nonesteamgame.ts:258` writes `getPath('exe')` in as the Steam shortcut's **launch command**), and `generateMacOsApp` carries the same risk in the `.app` wrapper's `run.sh` (`shortcuts.ts:227`) — **a wrong value is a semantic divergence between builds, not a stub gap**, and is only observable when the shortcut is clicked. **3 of the 7 are send-kind** (`addShortcut`, `processShortcut`, `removeShortcut`) and require the bidirectional registration-kind test. *(D-09, D-10)*
- [x] **REQ-34.5-06**: The **4 runner CLI version channels** are ported — `getLegendaryVersion`, `getGogdlVersion`, `getNileVersion`, `getCometVersion`. Pure version-probe reads; no seam dependency. **Records D-04's correction on the record: `getCometVersion` is GOG's, not Zoom's** — `comet` is the GOG Galaxy Communication replacement, gated on `gameInfo.runner === 'gog'` (`src/backend/launcher.ts:973`). An option label during the phase discussion wrongly bundled it with Zoom; Zoom is **exactly 3** channels, so the drop candidate set was 19, not 20. *(D-04)*
- [x] **REQ-34.5-07**: The **3 "other" channels** are ported — `callTool`, `egsSync`, `getGOGLinuxInstallersLangs`. **Answers CONTEXT.md's open gray area — none of the three hides an Electron dependency the stub does not cover**; they route only through already-baselined `dialog.ts`/`utils.ts`. `callTool` arrives here by reassignment from 34.1 (REQ-34.1-10 / 34.1 D-14) because it is Winetricks/winecfg/runExe — Wine tooling, this phase's domain. **`callTool`'s `winetricks` case already works** (`tools/ipc_handler.ts:31-33` calls `Winetricks.run()` on the shared `tools/index.ts` object — pure Node, no Electron) **despite the three winetricks IPC channels being deferred to 34.6**: flag it explicitly in the declared-channels doc, do **not** guard it behind a "wait for 34.6" comment. *(D-01, 34.1 D-14; research Pitfall 4)*
- [x] **REQ-34.5-08**: The **2 saves-sync channels** are ported — `syncSaves`, `syncGOGSaves`. **Resolves the Discretion question CONTEXT.md left open: `documents` belongs HERE**, not to shortcuts (`save_sync.ts:146`, reached via `syncGOGSaves`) — so this cluster is **also blocked on REQ-34.5-01**. Both runners' sync paths use non-interactive CLI flags; research verified **no dialog or prompt is reachable** from either, so D-15's fire-and-forget-rejection landmine does not apply here. The saves-sync **conflict-behavior** gray area CONTEXT.md deferred is out of scope: this is a byte-faithful port of inherited Electron behavior, not a redesign. *(D-09; research Pitfall 1)*
- [x] **REQ-34.5-09**: The **2 runtime channels** are ported — `downloadRuntime`, `isRuntimeInstalled`. Pure Node `axiosClient` download + tar extract to `runtimePath` (`toolsPath/runtimes`), which resolves through `pathShim`'s **already-implemented** `userData`; zero Electron dependency, no seam precondition. *(D-01)*
- [x] **REQ-34.5-10**: **The electron-reach ledger is extended and its baseline REGENERATED BY MEASUREMENT AT EXECUTION TIME — never transcribed from planning prose.** `src/backend/sidecar/__tests__/electronReachLedger.test.ts`'s `ENTRY_POINTS` gains this phase's new registration module(s). **Every prior slice's measured baseline disagreed with its own planning-time prediction at least once** (34.4 predicted two new entries and needed three), so a transcribed number is a defect, not a shortcut. `save_sync.ts` is a **confirmed new** entry (direct `electron` import, not yet baselined); several other 34.5-touched files (shortcuts, `legendary`/`gog`/`nile` user and library) are **ALREADY baselined** via prior slices' transitive reach and must **not** be re-added as if new. The ledger is growth-only, not strict-equality, so decoupling work never turns it red — and its baseline array **IS the Phase 35 cutover work-list**. *(34.3 D-10, 34.4 D-10 — standing obligation)*
- [x] **REQ-34.5-11**: **The deferral is TRACKED, not silently accepted — Phase 34.6 is inserted into `.planning/ROADMAP.md`** to port the 16 deferred channels (EOS overlay 8, SteamGridDB artwork 5, winetricks 3) **before Phase 35**. This is a hard requirement, not bookkeeping: **Phase 35's own roadmap entry requires the IPC re-plumb to be COMPLETE before cutover**, so an untracked deferral would silently become accepted loss — and this project's own history shows an open obligation is exactly how UAT debt accumulates. `IPC-PORT-INVENTORY.md` is updated so the 57 reconciles as 38 ported + 3 dropped + 16 moved, keeping the Phase 35 completion gate arithmetically checkable. `34.5-PORTED-CHANNELS.md` declares all 38 rows with port kind (`sidecar invoke` / `sidecar send`) and **honest proof level** — unit-proven, live-gate-proven, or ported-with-a-declared-caveat — following the 34.1-11/34.2-13/34.3-12/34.4-14/34.4.1-10 precedent. **Where an automated test proves only wiring, the artifact says so plainly: no reader may take "wired and unit-proven" for "seen working."** The 3 dropped and 16 deferred channels need **no new code** — the `UNPORTED_CHANNEL_MARKER` machinery already catches them at boot and declares them honestly; "drop" and "defer" mean *let it reject and say so*, not *build a degradation path*. *(D-02, D-03, D-05)*
- [x] **REQ-34.5-12**: A **BLOCKING 5-item live gate** under `pnpm tauri:dev` on macOS is a phase-closing condition, carrying 34.3 **D-13**, 34.4 **D-08** and 34.4.1 **D-09/D-10**. The items: **(1-3)** each of **Epic, GOG and Amazon** logs in **from scratch** and reaches a signed-in, **populated** library — all three, no "one live + two declared unit-proven-only"; item (3) additionally **captures one real redirect and confirms REQ-34.5-02's `www.amazon.com` anchor** against it; **(4)** `addToSteam` **and** the macOS `.app` shortcut both launch the correct binary, exercising **both** `exe` call sites and proving REQ-34.5-01's `GAMELIB_SHELL_EXE` mechanism end-to-end; **(5)** `runWineCommand` succeeds for at least one **non-Steam** runner, closing the never-proven gap. **Preconditions MUST be written as explicit numbered steps** — an unstated precondition is how a gate gets skipped and becomes UAT debt (Phases 21/30/34.2). Sidecar output is read from `~/Library/Logs/GameLib/gamelib.log`, **not** the `tauri:dev` terminal. **Gate failure is BLOCKING**: findings become a gap cycle inside 34.5. Winetricks has genuine macOS support, `listInstalled()`'s empty result is DATA-dependent, and no plan may write a gate step expecting a decline from a ported winetricks channel. D-10's winetricks disposition survives with its reasoning replaced: the `DECLARED_UNAVAILABLE_MARKER` capture is still owed but only BEFORE the port lands, as the gate's step 0. (2) **A-02 — D-05's EOS dialog fires at `remove()` (`eos_overlay.ts:162`, unconditional), not `enable()`** (`:197`, gated `if (!isInstalled())` and therefore unreachable in the install→enable→disable→remove order). The keyring hardening is therefore a CO-REQUISITE landing BEFORE that port, a narrow explicit exception to D-02's port-then-harden rule for this channel only; D-02 is unchanged for `importGame`/`moveInstall`/`runWineCommandForGame`. **A-04** corrects `IPC-PORT-INVENTORY.md`'s stale `GlobalState.tsx:1586` citation to `:1609`. **The clustering is RE-CLUSTERED from RESEARCH.md's 12-ID recommendation to 14** (RESEARCH's own assumption A2 declared the 12 a recommendation, not a constraint): REQ-34.6-13 was added because `34.6-PATTERNS.md` found THREE red-by-construction gates that CONTEXT.md and RESEARCH.md did not name (`wineToolsFlows.test.ts:133`'s absence assertion, `enrichmentFlows.test.ts`'s moving unported exemplar, and `electronReachLedger.test.ts`'s missing entry points), and REQ-34.6-14 was added because the phase's closing artefacts — `34.6-PORTED-CHANNELS.md`, the `IPC-PORT-INVENTORY.md` reconciliation and `U-34.5-32`'s two-part disposition — are deliverables no other requirement owned. `34.6-VALIDATION.md`'s Per-Task Verification Map is updated to match. Phase 34.8 (Frontend i18n compliance for fork-added code — retrofit, catalog tooling, enforcement gate). Minted 2026-08-07 during `/gsd-plan-phase 34.8` from 34.8-CONTEXT.md D-01..D-22, with 34.8-RESEARCH.md resolving the two decisions CONTEXT.md left open (D-16's gate mechanism → a new `ts-morph` AST scanner exercised as a `meta/` jest test, **zero new CI YAML**; and D-07's merge-base diff → a committed snapshot artifact, because `actions/checkout@v6` carries neither the Heroic upstream remote nor the merge-base commit). Each maps to Phase 34.8. **Two of ROADMAP.md's own named retrofit targets are resolved to NO CODE CHANGE by measurement, not by descope**: D-21 (ConsoleMode store labels, LogSettings titles and StoreSearch's `STORE_DISPLAY_NAME` are satisfied by extending the do-not-translate glossary) and D-22 (`PlatformSupport.tsx` is a measurement-superseded audit false positive — its only literals are exact glossary matches and its one prose string is already `t()`-wrapped). **The retrofit wave's size is DISCOVERED, not assumed**: 205 `src/frontend/**` files are touched since merge-base `b5b5cad3f` (104 net-new), the 2026-08-05 audit names examples with "etc.", and research's own sweep was time-boxed — so REQ-34.8-11 makes the gate's own audit-mode run the authoritative backlog generator. No ROADMAP ID was renumbered; the ROADMAP `Requirements: TBD` line is replaced by this set.

- [x] **REQ-34.8-01**: A **fork-owned `gamelib` namespace** carries every newly-retrofitted fork string — `public/locales/<locale>/gamelib.json`, called as two-argument `t('gamelib:<feature>.<slug>', 'English default')`, never a bare `t(key)`. The upstream Heroic catalogs (`translation`, `gamepage`, `login`) stay byte-identical so every future upstream sync merges cleanly. **No `ns` array is added to `i18next.init`** — namespaces already load lazily through `i18next-http-backend` behind the existing `<Suspense>` boundary, exactly as `gamepage` and `login` do today, and both Electron and Tauri serve the identical Vite-built `build/` tree (`frontendDist: "../build"`, no `publicDir` override). Because that reasoning is config-derived rather than observed, and because the `publicDir`/`getAppPath` divergence under the Tauri sidecar has bitten this project **four** times, closure requires a live both-runtime check in a **non-English** session: a missing `gamelib.json` is invisible in English, since every string silently falls back to its inline default. ROADMAP.md item (3) ("run `pnpm i18n` to sync the stale en catalogs") is **SUPERSEDED** — the deliverable is `en/gamelib.json` generated repeatably. *(D-01, D-04, Claude's Discretion on namespace registration)*
- [x] **REQ-34.8-02**: A machine-readable **do-not-translate glossary** (`meta/i18nGlossary.json`) pins brand, platform and unit terms literal: GameLib, Steam, Epic, GOG, Proton, CrossOver, Steam Deck, Linux, macOS, Windows, MB/s, **plus the D-21 compound and variant forms measured in live code** — Amazon, ZOOM, Zoom, Epic/Legendary, Amazon/Nile, Amazon Games. Matching is **exact, case-sensitive and whole-string**: `'Steam client not found'` is still a violation even though `'Steam'` is a term. Consequence, stated positively so it does not read as an omission: **`ConsoleMode`, `LogSettings` and `StoreSearch` need ZERO code change** — glossary coverage is the whole fix, and the gate failing on an unglossed brand name is the intended reminder to extend the list when a new store is added. *(D-02, D-21)*
- [x] **REQ-34.8-03**: `i18next-parser.config.js`'s **`keepRemoved` flips `false` → `true`**, pinned by an assertion test that fails loudly if it is ever reverted. This is not housekeeping: a live measured run on 2026-08-07 with `keepRemoved: false` **DELETED 36 real fork keys** (33 from `translation.json`, 3 from `gamepage.json`) that the static lexer cannot see through dynamic key construction and aliased `t` — churning exactly the ~50 upstream files this phase promises not to touch. The flip must land BEFORE any task runs `pnpm i18n`, or the run itself destroys fork data. Accepted cost: genuinely dead upstream keys stop being auto-pruned, harmless in a fork that is not the translation source of truth. *(D-03)*
- [x] **REQ-34.8-04**: The **no-churn guarantee is mechanical, not a promise**: `meta/i18nCatalogChurnGuard.ts` asserts that a `pnpm i18n` run changes nothing under `public/locales/` except `*/gamelib.json` (and its `*/gamelib.mt.json` provenance sidecar), and rides `pnpm test:ci`. This is the backstop that catches a mis-namespaced `t()` call silently writing into `translation.json`, and it is what makes D-06's split-brain and D-11's permissive translation-memory reads safe. On a violation the response is `git checkout -- public/locales/` and a diagnosis, never a hand-edit. *(D-05)*
- [x] **REQ-34.8-05**: The **split-brain is documented as intentional**, in `34.8-I18N-CONTRACT.md`. Fork keys already sitting in the upstream catalogs (80 Steam-mentioning lines in `en/translation.json`, 54 in `en/gamepage.json` — an upper bound including Heroic's own Steam-shortcut strings) **STAY where they are**; the `gamelib` namespace starts from this phase forward. Migrating them would require the very DELETIONS REQ-34.8-04 exists to block, so leaving them is what lets the byte-identical guarantee switch on immediately with no exception window. This is a deliberate non-action recorded in writing so it does not read as a bug later. *(D-06)*
- [x] **REQ-34.8-06**: Fork-owned frontend files are identified by **diffing against the upstream Heroic merge-base** (`b5b5cad3fa2e822602d320b70788d87240fc056e`, matching `package.json`'s `upstream.baseCommit`), via `meta/genI18nGateScope.ts` run OFFLINE and committing `meta/i18nGateScope.json`. **The diff must not run live in CI**: `actions/checkout@v6` gives a shallow, single-remote clone with no Heroic remote and no merge-base commit, so a live fetch would couple every PR to a third-party repo's reachability. Both `A` (net-new) and `M` (modified) statuses stay in scope — fork literals also live mixed into otherwise-upstream files. The snapshot records its `baseCommit`, so the next upstream rebase turns a test red rather than leaving the scope silently wrong; git is invoked with `execFileSync` on a fixed argv with the SHA regex-validated first. Rejected alternatives: git blame/authorship (the fork rewrote history across the Tauri phases) and the point-in-time 2026-08-05 manual audit. *(D-07)*
- [x] **REQ-34.8-07**: The enforcement gate is a **new `ts-morph` AST scanner** (`meta/hardcodedStringGate.ts`), resolving D-16 in research's favour over both offered options: `react/jsx-no-literals` is structurally blind to `.ts` files and to non-JSX literals like `copy.ts`'s `message:` properties, and `meta/lintTranslations.ts` is catalog-JSON-shaped with zero source-AST capability. `ts-morph` 17.0.1 is an installed devDependency with **no in-repo consumer**, so the algorithm comes from 34.8-RESEARCH.md § Architecture Patterns 1–5, not a codebase copy. Scope of "user-facing" is an explicit, exported, commented decision: JSX text plus the `title`/`aria-label`/`alt`/`placeholder`/`label` attributes are INCLUDED; `console.*` arguments are EXCLUDED (including them false-positives against the D-14-protected `repairFailure.ts`, whose design intent is that raw backend error text reaches the console and never a dialog), as are thrown `Error` messages, `data-*`, `className`/`id`/`key`/`type`/`role`, and every `aria-*` other than `aria-label`. Per D-13 the gate checks for hardcoded literals ONLY — **any `t()` call passes regardless of which namespace it targets**, which is what avoids false positives against the REQ-34.8-05 split-brain and against legitimate reuse of an upstream key. `scanScope()` refuses an empty scope and reports `totalCandidates`, so an unscanned scope can never read as a clean one. *(D-13, D-14, D-16)*
- [x] **REQ-34.8-08**: **The gate proves itself BEFORE it blocks.** `meta/__tests__/hardcodedStringGate.test.ts` carries NEGATIVE fixtures (a bare JSX literal, an object-property literal, a bare `return 'Unrated'`, each user-facing attribute, an interpolated template literal — every one must be reported) and POSITIVE fixtures for each D-14 idiom taken VERBATIM from the real file it protects: `[key, defaultText]` tuple tables (`CrossoverBadge`, `LibraryFilters`, and the third site research found — `Humble/Keys/stateLabels.ts`), aliased `t` (`t2`, `tr` — five confirmed call sites), `repairFailure.ts`'s deliberate English fallbacks, `bootErrorSurface.ts`'s comment-based full-file exemption, and every REQ-34.8-02 glossary term. **Each exemption carries a paired NEGATIVE case proving it is narrow** — a non-key-shaped two-string tuple is still two violations, a `let` string never passed to `t` is still a violation, a bare exemption marker with no reason does not exempt, and a near-miss containing a glossary term is still a violation. A gate that has not demonstrated both directions must not be flipped to blocking. *(D-12, D-14, D-16; 34.8-VALIDATION.md Wave 0)*
- [x] **REQ-34.8-09**: The gate is **BLOCKING in CI from day one — no advisory grace period**, and adds **zero new CI YAML**. It rides the existing `meta` jest project inside `pnpm test:ci`, already a blocking step in `.github/workflows/test.yml`, so a `meta/__tests__/*.test.ts` file IS a blocking check; `expect(violations).toHaveLength(0)` is the exit-code mechanism and no `process.exit` is added (`lintTranslations.ts` has none to copy). This is viable precisely because REQ-34.8-10 defers the two hard files behind an explicit allowlist, so everything the gate sees on day one is already compliant. An advisory gate nobody fails is the exact failure mode this phase exists to prevent — hardcoded constants already shipped once in the Steam features. The gate's own integrity is asserted alongside it: no `.skip`/`.todo`/`xit`, `totalCandidates > 0`, `scannedFiles` equal to the snapshot's file count, exactly 2 allowlist entries, and exactly one comment-exempt file. *(D-12, D-16, Claude's Discretion on which workflow hosts the gate)*
- [x] **REQ-34.8-10**: The two D-17 deferred files carry **allowlist entries with MEASURED expected violation counts that FAIL when stale** — `meta/i18nGateAllowlist.json`, `{file, expectedCount, reason}`. Staleness is **bidirectional**: a count that DROPS means the retrofit landed and the gate says `stale exemption — remove this entry`; a count that RISES means new hardcoded strings were added behind the exemption, which is exactly what the gate exists to stop. The exemption therefore cannot outlive its reason and nobody has to remember. `expectedCount` is measured by the gate itself, never transcribed from the ≈26–28 planning estimate, because that number IS the comparison basis. Chosen over a TODO comment (enforces nothing) and a ledger entry (a document cannot fail a build). `TauriLoginPanel.tsx` — the render half of the same feature — is already fully `t()`-wrapped and correctly needs no entry. *(D-17, D-18)*
- [x] **REQ-34.8-11**: **The retrofit backlog is generated by the gate's own audit-mode run, not assumed.** 34.8-AUDIT.md records the raw measurement, then triages EVERY violation into exactly one of four dispositions — `retrofit`, `glossary`, `file-exemption`, `deferred` — with no unclassified bucket, and closes each assigned item as `closed` / `reclassified` / `blocked`. This supersedes both the 2026-08-05 manual audit (point-in-time, names examples with "etc.", cannot drive scope) and 34.8-RESEARCH.md's own time-boxed heuristic sweep. `appleRating.ts:31`'s bare `'Unrated'` is a known-answer control the report must contain — its absence means the scanner missed a real violation and the gate is not yet trustworthy. **If the backlog exceeds 40 violations or 12 files, a blocking developer decision fires**; none of its three options drops work, and the option that would defer a remainder must record in writing that it weakens REQ-34.8-09. Suppressing a violation by widening the allowlist instead of fixing it is explicitly forbidden. *(D-07, D-19; research Pitfall 4, Open Question 4, Assumption A3)*
- [x] **REQ-34.8-12**: `src/frontend/components/UI/RedeemSteamKeyDialog/copy.ts` — the module that bypasses i18n entirely — is retrofitted to the **injected-`TFunction` idiom**, `redeemOutcomeCopy(outcome, t, packageName?)`, mirroring `repairFailure.ts`. This is a behavioural choice, not a style one: the competing idiom (`GlobalStateV2.ts`'s module-scope `import { t } from 'i18next'`) is not guaranteed to have a lazily-loaded namespace resolved at call time, whereas the caller's `t` is Suspense-gated — and `redeemOutcomeCopy()` runs during render and event handling. Every English string is preserved VERBATIM as the `t()` default (a routing change, not a copy edit), the `success` branch's template literal becomes a `{{packageName}}` interpolation with a distinct key, and a throwing `t` still yields a rendered English dialog. The existing `__tests__/copy.test.ts` stays green and gains a copy-preservation case and a throwing-`t` case. *(D-19; 34.8-PATTERNS.md recommendation over its own flagged gap)*
- [x] **REQ-34.8-13**: `src/frontend/bootErrorSurface.ts` gets **an exemption comment, not a retrofit** — it renders the renderer's bootstrap-failure surface and runs before `i18next.init()` completes, so no `t()` call there could resolve; the whole deliverable for that file is one `i18n-gate-exempt:` line carrying its reason (a bare marker does not exempt), and none of its six strings is touched. `PlatformSupport.tsx` is **DROPPED from the retrofit** — measured, its only literals are `title="Windows"`/`"macOS"`/`"Linux"`, exact glossary matches, and its one prose string is already `t()`-wrapped, so its appearance in the 2026-08-05 audit list, in ROADMAP.md and in D-19 is a **measurement-superseded false positive**. Both non-actions are recorded in `34.8-I18N-CONTRACT.md` so neither reads as an omission at verification time. Rejected: wrapping the OS names for stylistic consistency, which would contradict D-02's pinning them literal. *(D-14, D-19, D-22)*
- [x] **REQ-34.8-14**: `meta/lintTranslations.ts` becomes **`gamelib`-aware**, and its CI-facing invocation is **scoped to `gamelib.json` only** (`LINT_TRANSLATIONS_NAMESPACES`, env-var driven because the script is invoked through an `esbuild | node` pipe where argv does not reach it). A bare `pnpm lint-translations` keeps its existing all-namespace behaviour unchanged — D-15 scopes the newly-CI-wired invocation, not the script's general-purpose behaviour. The reason is signal quality: running the existing empty-string / extra-key / content-less-tag checks across all 49 locales would surface a wall of pre-existing upstream failures on Weblate-sourced data the fork does not own and cannot fix, and a gate with unactionable noise stops being read. `en` is not linted for empty strings — an extracted-but-empty key is CORRECT, since `defaultValue: ''` plus `returnEmptyString: false` plus `fallbackLng: 'en'` is what makes the inline `t(key, 'Default')` fallback work. *(D-15)*
- [x] **REQ-34.8-15**: The **glossary-aware machine-fill script ships BUILT AND PROVEN, deliberately NOT bulk-run** (`meta/machineFillGamelib.ts`, `pnpm machine-fill-gamelib`). Contract: fills only keys missing or empty in a locale, **never overwrites a non-empty value** (which is what lets human corrections survive re-runs), preserves `{{interpolation}}` placeholders and `_one`/`_other` plural siblings, passes the dotted key path as context, and treats English as the only source of truth; a translation failing validation is left UNFILLED and reported rather than written. MT provenance is a per-locale sidecar manifest (`public/locales/<locale>/gamelib.mt.json`) chosen over a commit-message convention because provenance must survive rebases and squashes and be readable by the script at runtime — and it is what lets a future Weblate import flag MT entries as "needs editing" rather than approved human translation. The existing upstream catalogs are fed in as **read-only translation memory** so shared vocabulary matches whatever Heroic already uses in that locale. Proven on TWO locales with an idempotence re-run and a `pnpm test:ci` parity test that will cover the remaining 46 automatically; a bulk 48-locale run is REFUSED without an explicit typed opt-in, because any script bug in a bulk run ships to 48 files at once. The API key is read from the environment only, never a CLI flag (the `verifyUpdaterSigningKey.ts` convention), and the model ID is chosen current at implementation time and env-overridable, never transcribed stale. *(D-08, D-09, D-10, D-11)*

Phase 34.10 (Navigation shell — horizontal card tabs replace the sidebar). Minted 2026-08-07 during `/gsd-plan-phase 34.10` from 34.10-CONTEXT.md D-01..D-13 + 34.10-UI-SPEC.md (checker-approved 6/6) + 34.10-RESEARCH.md + 34.10-PATTERNS.md + 34.10-VALIDATION.md. Each maps to Phase 34.10. **ROADMAP.md's own "Known constraints" list is superseded on one point**: its 78px macOS traffic-light reserve is a CONDITIONAL statement whose condition is false on this branch — `framelessWindow` defaults to `false` (`src/backend/config.ts:367`), `tauri.conf.json`/`main.rs` set no `titleBarStyle`/`decorations`, and Tauri's `setDecorations(false)` removes macOS traffic lights entirely, so the real inset today is **0px** (CONTEXT D-01, independently reconfirmed by RESEARCH). **Three silent-breakage traps are minted as requirements rather than left as execution notes**, because each one passes a green test suite while being broken: the `.Sidebar` drag-region fallback (REQ-34.10-04), the static `meta/i18nGateScope.json` snapshot (REQ-34.10-14) and `HeroicVersion`'s mount-timing side effects (REQ-34.10-12). **A fourth trap was found at planning time and is not in any upstream artifact**: `LibrarySearchBar`/`CategoryFilter`/`LibraryFilters` all consume `LibraryContext`, whose `initialContext` is a full set of silent no-op setters — rendering `<Header>` in the app-level shell instead of inside `Library`'s provider would produce visible-but-dead filter controls with zero errors, so REQ-34.10-09 mandates a React portal rather than a component move.

- [x] **REQ-34.10-01**: The left sidebar is replaced by a **two-tier navigation shell** mounted at `src/frontend/App.tsx`'s single `<Sidebar />` site: tier 1 is a horizontal row of four route-driven card/folder tabs (`Manage Accounts` → `/login`, `Games` → `/`, `Stores` → `/store/:store`, `Settings` → `/settings/:type`, in that order), tier 2 is a vertical contextual panel scoped to the active tab. `App.css`'s grid becomes `'offline offline' 'navbar navbar' 'tier2 content' 'tier2 controller'`. **The `.App` and `.App .content` selector NAMES are load-bearing and must survive verbatim** — `src/frontend/screens/Login/__tests__/index.test.tsx`'s F-10 regression gate text-parses both blocks by literal selector string and throws `unterminated block` rather than failing meaningfully if either is renamed; `min-height: 100%` must not return to `.App .content`. Console mode (`App.tsx`'s `isConsoleMode` branch) renders bare with no shell, exactly as it renders no sidebar today. No route-table change: the two tiers are a presentational layer over the existing flat `createHashRouter` table. *(whole-phase scope; D-01..D-13)*
- [x] **REQ-34.10-02**: Tier 1 reuses MUI `<Tabs>`/`<Tab>` (already a dependency, already rendered at `WineManager/index.tsx:222`) **for its accessibility scaffolding only** — `role="tablist"`/`role="tab"`/`aria-selected`, roving `tabIndex`, arrow-key nav, and `id="tab-{index}"` matching `components/UI/TabPanel/index.tsx:14`'s existing `aria-labelledby="tab-{index}"` contract — and is **fully reskinned**: MUI's default underline indicator is suppressed, because "underline + sliding indicator" is the explicitly rejected alternative from sketch 001. Each `<Tab>` is route-driven via `component={NavLink}` + `to`, not `value`/`onChange` state (WineManager's usage switches a data source, not a URL, and must not be copied literally). The active tab's "merge into the content surface" seam is defined **relationally** — active background is `var(--body-background)`, the seam-erasing token, never `var(--navbar-active-background)` (which is reserved for active tier-2 rows) and never a literal colour. Zero new icon assets: `faUserAlt`/`faGamepad`/`faStore`/`faSlidersH` are already imported by the file being retired. Wordmark stays **icon-only at ~28–32px** (UI-SPEC OQ5); no search icon and no avatar are added to `nav-right` (UI-SPEC OQ6) — neither maps to a real GameLib feature. *(UI-SPEC Locked Inputs, D-04)*
- [x] **REQ-34.10-03**: The navbar's leading inset is a **conditional token defaulting to 0px**, not a hardcoded 78px, and this phase **does not adopt** Tauri's `titleBarStyle: Overlay`/`hiddenTitle`. The sketches' 78px reserve assumes an overlaid macOS titlebar this branch never produces. The token is declared once so that adopting overlay mode later is a value change rather than a re-layout. Adopting overlay mode here would be a window-chrome change riding along inside a navigation phase, and 34.1's frameless work already produced pre-paint/decoration defects (CR-02). *(D-01; supersedes ROADMAP's stale framing)*
- [x] **REQ-34.10-04**: **The window drag region migrates off `.Sidebar` in the same commit as the test that pins it.** `src/preload/api/tauriWindowChrome.ts:333` returns `start.closest('.Sidebar') !== null`, reached only when every ancestor's computed `-webkit-app-region` is empty — which is the **active** path under WKWebView, since macOS's webview does not implement the property. The selector becomes the tier-1 navbar row's class; the existing interactive-element exclusion (`a, button, [role="button"], input, select, textarea, .windowControls`) is preserved verbatim, so tabs, the Downloads ring and the window controls stay no-drag while empty navbar space drags. `src/preload/__tests__/framelessRuntime.test.ts`'s **three** `FakeElement('div', { className: 'Sidebar' })` fixtures (the two drag/no-drag cases plus the WR-01 `.windowControls` regression guard) are updated in lockstep — the fixture's `className` is test-controlled fiction, so leaving `'Sidebar'` there after the source change would leave three tests passing while proving nothing about the real navbar. A decorated (non-frameless) window keeps **no** app-provided drag surface; its native titlebar already provides one. *(D-02)* **RE-CONFIRMED Complete 2026-08-08 (plan 34.10-22, live gate run 3, item 2, PASS):** all five sub-checks PASS. Run 3 deliberately refused to carry this item forward on the source-level argument that `resolveDragRegion` was untouched by the gap cycle — because 34.10-18 had already committed a fix to this same `.NavShell__navbar` element on exactly that reasoning and been wrong in 9 of 11 themes. The re-measurement cost under a minute and converts four "should NOT move" exclusions from inference into observation; notably the drag-by-a-tab exclusion held despite 34.10-19 changing `.MuiTab-root`'s `min-height`, confirming live what source review had only argued. See `34.10-LIVE-GATE.md` run 3, Item 2.
- [x] **REQ-34.10-05**: **Tauri is the design and verification target; Electron must keep working, not look perfect.** Under Electron the new shell renders and navigates correctly, but cosmetic imperfection is **recorded, not fixed** — specifically that Electron's `titleBarStyle: 'hidden'` + `titleBarOverlay: true` (`src/backend/main_window.ts:45-49`) *keeps* macOS traffic lights, unlike Tauri's `setDecorations(false)`, so a frameless Electron user may see them overlap the tab bar. Phase 35 deletes Electron; effort spent making it pixel-correct is effort spent on a build being removed. This is a deliberate non-action written down so it does not read as an omission at verification time. *(D-03)* **REVERTED to Pending 2026-08-08 (plan 34.10-11, live gate item 5):** the shell-renders sub-check and the traffic-light observation (NO overlap) both PASSed, but the "navigates correctly" claim's own tab-navigation sub-check was recorded NOT ATTEMPTED, never inferred, per this project's standing rule — this checkbox was marked Complete during plan 34.10-08 from unit tests alone, before the live gate this requirement's own text depends on ever ran. See `34.10-LIVE-GATE.md` Item 5. **RE-COMPLETED 2026-08-08 (plan 34.10-16, live gate run 2, item 5, PASS):** shell renders AND all four tier-1 tabs navigate, both measured live for the first time — the tab-navigation sub-check that was NOT ATTEMPTED in run 1 is now closed. The traffic-light divergence (NO overlap under Electron) remains recorded, not fixed, per D-03. All three of this requirement's own claims are now satisfied on a measured run. See `34.10-LIVE-GATE.md` run 2, Item 5. **RE-CONFIRMED Complete 2026-08-08 (plan 34.10-22, live gate run 3, item 5, PASS):** shell renders and all four tier-1 tabs navigate under Electron. Re-run because `App.css` and `NavShell/index.scss` — both shared unconditionally by whichever shell mounts the React tree — changed in 34.10-18, so the question "do the shared CSS changes render acceptably under Chromium" was genuinely open even though no Electron-specific code path was touched. Run 1's traffic-light observation (NO overlap) stands and was not re-taken, per D-03's record-don't-fix framing. Recorded honestly: P3's required pre-launch single-instance re-check was executed immediately *after* the operator's report rather than before it, so the contract's sequencing was not achieved; the check nonetheless found both `pgrep` probes empty, so the two shells never ran concurrently and the evidence is scorable. See `34.10-LIVE-GATE.md` run 3, Item 5.
- [x] **REQ-34.10-06**: **Theme survival is a blocking gate on three themes, verified visually on a live build.** `midnightMirage` (default, navbar `#161c1e` over body `#070a0b`), `gruvbox_dark` (`#32302f` over `#282828`) and `dracula`/`dracula-classic` (`#44475a` over `#282a35` — the highest inversion, navbar much LIGHTER than body) must each show the active tab reading as continuous with the content surface. Active states are defined relationally (tab background == content background), never as "the navbar is the dark band". **Never evaluate the nav in `midnightMirage` alone** — two of the three sketch themes exist purely as this stress test, and the card/folder tab style was selected on theme survival rather than looks. This cannot be automated: the `Frontend` jest project has no jsdom, no rendering and no screenshot capability. *(D-04)* **REVERTED to Pending 2026-08-08 during gap cycle 2 planning:** `34.10-LIVE-GATE.md` run 2, Item 1 is recorded FAIL — three defects found on the same live measurement this requirement's own text depends on: F-34.10-03 (a visible ~10px seam), F-34.10-04 (the wordmark and Downloads ring reading as a second row below the tab strip) and F-34.10-06 (the navbar scrolls away and the scrollbar draws over it). The per-theme sweep across `midnightMirage`, `gruvbox_dark`, `dracula` and the `dracula-classic` spot-check was recorded NOT ATTEMPTED in run 2 and has therefore never been measured in ANY run. **STAYS PENDING after plan 34.10-19 (2026-08-08):** 34.10-19 implemented F-34.10-04's source-level fix (min-height override) and its own gates all pass, but its own `<verification>` section is explicit that "nothing here is proven by the suite" and "do not tick REQ-34.10-06 from anything in this plan" — the `Frontend` jest project has no jsdom/CSS engine, so no test can see whether the wordmark, tab strip and Downloads ring share a line. The adjudicator remains plan 34.10-22's live gate. See `34.10-19-SUMMARY.md`. **STAYS PENDING after live gate run 3 (plan 34.10-22, 2026-08-08, item 1 FAIL):** item 1 failed on two independent grounds. (1) F-34.10-03 and F-34.10-04 both still FAIL — operator's verbatim words "still a gap" and "gamelib icon and download sit lower" — measured against a bundle verified BEFORE scoring to contain 34.10-18's seam border, its token correction, and 34.10-19's tab `min-height`; the fixes landed and did not work. F-34.10-04's shape has changed, from run 2's full wrap onto a second row to a vertical misalignment, recorded as an observation with no cause assigned. (2) All three scored themes FAILED the seam column. **The per-theme sweep this requirement's own text demands was nevertheless COMPLETED for the first time in ANY run** — `midnightMirage`, `gruvbox_dark` and `dracula` each carry a distinct seam and idle-ring verdict, resolving a column recorded NOT ATTEMPTED in both prior runs; `dracula-classic` is NOT ATTEMPTED for a concrete reason, namely that the theme does not exist in the shipped picker, which is a defect in run 3's own contract rather than an operator omission. The sweep's own finding is recorded as an observation, not a diagnosis: the seam is **identical across all three themes**, including `dracula`, whose navbar/body inversion makes it the likeliest to diverge. The gamepad focus-scroll regression is NOT ATTEMPTED (no controller available) and is carried forward as unmeasured, never inferred from the mouse/keyboard result — different code path, different arithmetic. See `34.10-LIVE-GATE.md` run 3, Item 1. **COMPLETE — closed by live gate run 4 (plan 34.10-27, 2026-08-09, verdict PASS 5/5).** Item 1 PASSES. The per-theme sweep this requirement's own text demands was completed across `midnightMirage`, `gruvbox_dark` and `dracula`, with the seam cell and the idle-ring cell for each theme elicited as six separate answers — the first run in this phase with clean per-theme provenance on all six. F-34.10-03 and F-34.10-04 are CLOSED BY MEASUREMENT, and specifically by measurement **in a scored theme**: commit `220211230`'s pixel verification and the operator's `1c7a3359d` confirmation were both taken in an unscored teal scheme, so gap cycle 3 deliberately refused to close them on a shipped fix — this requirement had already been ticked-then-reverted once, and the same truth was declared fixed twice before and contradicted twice. `dracula-classic` is permanently DROPPED from scope (F-34.10-07: it does not exist in the shipped theme picker); this requirement's text above still names it and is left UNREWORDED by design, per the binding operator decision to gate the card/folder tab as-is. **PERMANENT RESIDUAL RISK, carried forward rather than closed:** the gamepad focus-scroll regression has been NOT ATTEMPTED in all four runs of this gate (no controller available, P9). `GamesList/index.tsx:46-76`'s `scrollCardIntoView` — container-rect-relative arithmetic, exercised only when `activeController` is truthy, and directly affected by plan 34.10-18's scroll-container relocation off `document.body` — has therefore **never been measured in any run of this phase**, and must never be inferred from the mouse/keyboard back-to-top sub-check's PASS: different code path, different arithmetic. This is recorded as a named residual risk, not a pending gate cell.

The now-dead frameless padding hack at `Header/index.css:14-18` (`.frameless:not(.fullscreen) .Header`) is **removed**, not left to rot, since `Header` no longer sits under the window controls. Count comes from `elements.length` on the same `window.api.getDMQueueInformation()` / `handleDMQueueInformation` subscription currently plumbed at `Sidebar/index.tsx:26-44`. An aggregate progress bar is explicitly rejected: per-item sizes vary enough that it would move unevenly and sometimes backwards, and no aggregate source exists in the codebase to reuse. **The ring is always present — dimmed and unfilled when idle.** This inverts today's behaviour (`CurrentDownload/index.tsx:50-52` returns `null` when `libraryStatus.length === 0`); holding the slot stops an element popping in and out of a fixed-height navbar and makes Downloads reliably findable rather than existing only mid-download. *(D-06, D-07)* **REVERTED to Pending 2026-08-08 (plan 34.10-11, live gate item 4, FAIL):** F-34.10-02 — no ring renders at rest (invisible, not "dimmed and unfilled" as required above), the fill never leaves 0turn during a real download, and the idle/active states were confirmed only by unit tests (`testEnvironment: 'node'`, no jsdom) that cannot resolve a CSS variable, compute contrast, or observe a hook returning no percent against a live queue. The two risks 34.10-15-SUMMARY.md flagged as unresolved (a `key`-driven remount possibly undercutting the fix; H3's mechanism possibly producing only a transient frame) are FALSIFIED by this measurement. See `34.10-LIVE-GATE.md` run 2, Item 4. **RE-CONFIRMED Complete 2026-08-08 (plan 34.10-22, live gate run 3, item 4, PASS):** sub-checks (ii)-(vi) all PASS, each re-driven against a real download rather than silently inherited from run 2, and the cross-referenced stand-in for sub-check (i) — item 1's `midnightMirage` idle-ring cell — also PASSED, so this item's second gating condition is satisfied rather than assumed. The reopening trigger written into run 3's scope ("if item 1's per-theme idle-ring sweep surfaces ANY FAIL, reopen item 4 in full") did **not** fire: all three scored idle-ring cells PASS, and this requirement's "always present — dimmed and unfilled when idle" clause is therefore measured across three themes for the first time. Sub-check (vi), the `ProgressHeader` percent discriminator, was re-asked explicitly rather than accepting an obvious operator typo, because it is the single cell distinguishing a genuinely-tracking ring from the `--dl-progress` frozen-at-`0turn` failure that already shipped once in this phase behind a fully green suite; confirmed advancing past 0%. See `34.10-LIVE-GATE.md` run 3, Item 4.
- [x] **REQ-34.10-09**: The **Games tier-2 panel carries today's filter controls across unchanged** — `LibrarySearchBar`, `CategoryFilter`, `LibraryFilters`, stacked in sketch 004 variant C's order (search → views → collections → facets) so Phase 34.11 swaps contents in place. `LibraryHeader` (game count, sync status, `ActionIcons`, `AddGameButton`) and the horizontal `<AlphabetFilter>` strip **stay above the grid**. The `Header` wrapper loses `position: sticky` and its horizontal `space-between` layout to become a vertical stack; **the three controls keep their own styling — restyling them to fit the column is 34.11's job**, deliberately not done twice. **The relocation is a React portal, not a component move**: all three controls consume `LibraryContext` (`frontend/screens/Library/LibraryContext.tsx`), whose `initialContext` supplies a complete set of no-op setters, so rendering them outside `Library`'s provider yields visible-but-dead filters with no error, no warning and no failing test. `Library` therefore portals `<Header />` into a shell-owned tier-2 target, keeping the controls inside the provider's React tree while placing them in the shell's DOM. `Library`'s `--header-height` measurement effect (which sizes `.libraryHeader`'s sticky offset from `.Header`'s rendered height) is retired with it. *(D-08, D-09; planning-time trap not present in any upstream artifact)* **REVERTED to Pending 2026-08-08 (plan 34.10-11, live gate item 3, FAIL):** F-34.10-01 — the portal seam itself is CONFIRMED correct (search filters the grid live, proving `<Header />` really is inside `LibraryContext.Provider` and the no-op `initialContext` trap this requirement exists to prevent did NOT ship), but `CategoryFilter` and `LibraryFilters` are unusable in the 204px column: `Dropdown/index.scss`'s `right:0; min-width:250px` popup overflows off the left edge of the window, and `popUpOnHover` opens it on mouseover instead of click. But a NEW defect in the same relocated controls was found in the same run: **F-34.10-05**, a black background that breaks the disclosure panel's styling. Run 1's own FAIL on this requirement was attributed to exactly this class of defect (the controls' own geometry/rendering breaking down in the narrow column, not the portal wiring) — by that same standard, a rendering defect in the same component is within this requirement's scope, not deferred to 34.11's restyling work (which covers *fitting* the existing look to the column, not a broken background that ignores the theme chain entirely). Left Pending rather than ticked, pending a fix for F-34.10-05.
