That third theme matters to this tick: run 3 deliberately excluded `midnightMirage` from scoring because `--background` and `--navbar-background` coincide there, making the original defect invisible by construction, so three independent themes passing is what rules out a fix that only holds where those tokens happen to agree. The run-2 precedent is honoured rather than quietly reversed — a rendering defect in these relocated controls was and remains inside this requirement's scope, not deferred to 34.11. Nothing here is ticked from a test: the `Frontend` jest project is `testEnvironment: 'node'` with no jsdom and no CSS engine. See `34.10-LIVE-GATE.md` run 3, Item 3.
- [x] **REQ-34.10-10**: **Tier-2 width is a single `--tier2-width` token, value 204px**, protected by a source-gate test asserting exactly one declaration across the frontend stylesheets. The two sketch reference files conflict — `navigation-shell.md:59,156` says 204px, `library-filtering.md:109-111` says 218px — and 204px wins because it is the value the navigation sketch's own layout, collapse transition and content `min-width: 0` rules were all built and judged against; 218px appears only in the *filter* sketch's panel CSS, which is 34.11's scope to retune. `_spacing.scss` has **no width-token precedent** (its scale is exclusively em-relative gaps and paddings), so this is a new token shape declared alongside the shell's layout rather than forced into the `spacingSystem` mixin — 204px is a content-fit pixel value, not typographic rhythm. Manage Accounts is the **only** tab that collapses the panel (width/padding/opacity → 0, border-right transparent); the resulting ~204px content-width shift on that one infrequent tab is a recorded decision with rationale and **must not be "fixed"** — the fix (always-populated panels) permanently narrows the Games grid. *(D-10; sketch `navigation-shell.md` §5)*
- [x] **REQ-34.10-11**: **All 14 sidebar destinations are redistributed with their guards intact, and none is silently dropped.** Tier-2 Stores: GOG, Steam, Epic and Amazon store routes, Deals, Store Search, Humble Keys (guard `humble?.isLoggedIn`), Redeem a Steam key (guard `steam.username`, rendered as a `<button>` calling `handleRedeemKeyDialog(true)`, never a link). Tier-2 Settings: General, Game Defaults (guard `!isWin`), Advanced, Wine Manager (guard `!isWin`), Accessibility, Console Mode, Log, **System Information appended after Log**, Documentation, Ko-fi (a `<button>` routed through the external-link-dialog gate), and `QuitButton` at the panel's bottom next to the version display. **Wine Manager, Accessibility and Console Mode are REPARENTED, not relocated** — all three are currently top-level `SidebarItem`s (siblings of Settings), not members of the existing Settings submenu, so this is component-tree restructuring with new guard placement. The Downloads nav entry is removed as a *list item* only; its `/download-manager` route is untouched and remains the ring's target. **ZOOM Platform stays absent** despite `SidebarLinks/index.tsx:161-167` still rendering it conditionally today — standing decision, not an oversight. The Stores tab's own target keeps the existing `defaultStore` cascade (`SidebarLinks/index.tsx:82-113`, including the `sessionStorage['last-store']` override) unchanged, and the Games tab keeps the library-refresh side effect the old GameLib item fired on click. The sidebar's `localStorage['sidebar-width']` drag-resize (`Sidebar/index.tsx:65-136`) is **not** carried forward; the `window.api.handleGoToScreen` navigation subscription registered at `Sidebar/index.tsx:58-63` **is** — losing it would silently break tray/deeplink navigation. *(D-12; ROADMAP settled structure)*
- [x] **REQ-34.10-12**: **`HeroicVersion` moves intact AND stays mounted unconditionally.** It is not a version label: it clears the cache on a version change (`HeroicVersion/index.tsx:33-42`) and hosts the `ChangelogModal` that auto-shows on startup (`:65-77`). Both effects are mount-triggered, so its visual home in the Settings tier-2 footer must **not** be its mount condition — if it mounted only while the Settings tab were active, the cache-clear and startup changelog would fire only after a user visited Settings rather than on every launch, a silent regression from today's always-mounted sidebar footer that no existing test covers. The shell therefore mounts it once, unconditionally, and hides only its visual block when Settings is not the active tab; the `ChangelogModal` renders through MUI's portal and is unaffected by that hiding. This resolves 34.10-RESEARCH.md Open Question 1 explicitly rather than letting mount timing fall out of component placement. Its `TourButton` entry point is removed with the tour (REQ-34.10-13); nothing else about the component changes — **it is moved, not split**. *(D-11; RESEARCH Trap 5 / Open Question 1)*
- [x] **REQ-34.10-13**: **The onboarding tour is DISABLED by this phase and its rework is a separate phase.** `SidebarTour` is not mounted, not renamed and not rebuilt; its twelve `data-tour="sidebar-*"` selectors are string-anchored and would resolve to nothing once the sidebar is retired, so leaving it live ships silently broken steps. The `TourButton` inside `HeroicVersion` — the tour's *other* entry point, which would otherwise start an unanchored tour from the Settings footer — is removed too. `SidebarTour.tsx` itself is **preserved on disk, unmounted**, because it plus its selector set is the deferred tour-rework phase's work-list. That phase needs a ROADMAP entry. A pre-existing duplicate `data-tour="sidebar-downloads"` value (`Sidebar/index.tsx:142` and `SidebarLinks/index.tsx:267` set it on two different elements) is recorded here for that phase; it is moot while the tour is off. *(D-13)*
- [x] **REQ-34.10-14**: **i18n compliance is proven against a REGENERATED gate scope, not an inherited one.** Every relocated label reuses its existing key verbatim across 49 locale directories — this is a reparenting migration, not a text edit — and **only the "Games" tab label is genuinely new** (`t('nav.tabs.games', 'Games')`, default namespace, using the plain `useTranslation()` `t` already in scope; no `gamelib:` prefix, since it is a first-party nav label shaped like its `stores`/`Settings` siblings, and no third aliased hook name is introduced). The blocking trap: `meta/i18nGateScope.json` is a **static committed snapshot** regenerated manually by `pnpm gen-i18n-gate-scope`, not a live glob — every brand-new nav component file is **invisible** to the blocking hardcoded-string gate until it is regenerated and committed, so a raw string literal in a new file would pass `pnpm test:ci` green today and surface only at some future regeneration. This phase therefore runs `pnpm gen-i18n-gate-scope` after its new files exist, verifies each new path appears in the regenerated `files` array, and only then treats a green `pnpm test:ci` as proof the gate scanned the new code. Pre-existing unrelated drift in the three upstream `en/` catalogs (carried forward from 34.8) is **not** fixed here; a churn-guard failure must be attributed to that drift before proceeding, never worked around by editing an upstream catalog. *(D-04 ROADMAP i18n constraint; UI-SPEC Copywriting Contract; RESEARCH Trap 3 — not present in any prior 34.10 artifact)*
- [x] **REQ-34.10-15**: **The retired components' test coverage is replaced, not deleted.** `SidebarLinks/__tests__/index.test.tsx` pins the flat list this phase removes (`labels[0] === 'GameLib'`, "Manage Accounts immediately before Accessibility") and directly imports the module being deleted, so it is a compile error rather than a failing assertion — it needs full replacement against the new shell components, preserving its real behavioural claims: Redeem-a-Steam-key gating on `steam.username` including the `handleRedeemKeyDialog(true)` click, Manage Accounts pointing at `/login` in both logged-in and logged-out states and never rendering a "Login" label, and no Discord / GitHub Sponsors while Ko-fi remains. New tests additionally cover `HeroicVersion` staying mounted for every active tab and `SidebarTour` never mounting. **Every new frontend test uses this repo's structural direct-invoke idiom** — mock the consumed hooks, stub CSS-importing children, call the component as a plain function, walk the returned React-element graph. `render()`, `screen.getBy*` and `userEvent` are **unexecutable in this repo**: the `Frontend` jest project is `testEnvironment: 'node'` and neither `jest-environment-jsdom` nor `react-test-renderer` is installed. Baseline to hold: 204 suites / 4069 tests, plus this phase's additions, zero regressions. *(34.10-VALIDATION.md hard constraint; RESEARCH Testing Impact)*
- [x] **REQ-34.10-16**: **A blocking live gate on a real Tauri build closes the phase — a green suite does not.** Two behaviours are unprovable by any jest test in this repo and are executed and recorded by hand before `/gsd-verify-work`: (1) the card-tab merge illusion across `midnightMirage`, `gruvbox_dark` and `dracula`/`dracula-classic` (REQ-34.10-06), and (2) real window dragging by the new navbar under WKWebView with `framelessWindow` toggled both ways (REQ-34.10-04) — the unit test mocks `closest()` and therefore proves only the JS branch, never the webview's actual behaviour. The project lesson is on the record: **a live gate beat a green suite three times.** The gate also records the accepted Electron cosmetic divergence (REQ-34.10-05) rather than leaving it unobserved. Any item not measured is recorded NOT ATTEMPTED, never inferred. *(D-02, D-03, D-04; 34.10-VALIDATION.md Manual-Only rows)* **STAYS PENDING after live gate run 2 (plan 34.10-16, 2026-08-08):** run 2 scored 4/5 (items 2, 3, 4, 5 PASS) but item 1 — the card-tab merge illusion, this requirement's own first named behaviour — FAILED on two new defects (F-34.10-03 visible seam gap; F-34.10-04 logo/ring wrapping to a second row) plus a related F-34.10-06 (navbar not fixed to the top; scrollbar draws over it). The gate mechanism itself performed exactly as this requirement demands — every item was honestly measured and recorded, NOT ATTEMPTED used where true rather than inferred — but this requirement's own text is that the gate **closes the phase**, and the phase has not closed while item 1 remains FAIL. See `34.10-LIVE-GATE.md` run 2. **STAYS PENDING after live gate run 3 (plan 34.10-22, 2026-08-08):** run 3 scored **4/5** — items 2, 3, 4 and 5 PASS, item 1 FAILS — and this requirement's own text is that the gate *closes the phase*, which requires 5/5. 4/5 has now failed to close this phase twice. Of this requirement's two named unprovable behaviours, (2) real window dragging under WKWebView (REQ-34.10-04) PASSED all five sub-checks, re-driven live rather than carried forward; (1) the card-tab merge illusion (REQ-34.10-06) FAILED. The accepted Electron divergence (REQ-34.10-05) was again recorded, not fixed, per D-03. The gate mechanism itself again performed as this requirement demands, and did more than score: its preflight caught that the running app was serving a **pre-fix bundle** (`frontendDist` points at a static directory, so a bare `tauri dev` serves whatever was last built) — had that gone unnoticed, run 3 would have recorded FAIL on all four findings against fixes the running app never contained. It also completed the per-theme sweep and item 3's (vi)/(vii) sub-checks, all three outstanding since run 1. Six ways in which run 3's own automated checks were unsatisfiable against a correctly-filled specimen are recorded as F-34.10-07; none altered a measured verdict. See `34.10-LIVE-GATE.md` run 3 and §9.6. **COMPLETE — closed by live gate run 4 (plan 34.10-27, 2026-08-09).** Run 4 scored **5/5** and this requirement's own text is that the gate closes the phase; 4/5 had failed to close it twice. Both named unprovable behaviours PASS: (1) the card-tab merge illusion (REQ-34.10-06) across all three scored themes, and (2) real window dragging under WKWebView (REQ-34.10-04), all five sub-checks re-driven live rather than carried forward. The accepted Electron divergence (REQ-34.10-05) was again recorded, not fixed, per D-03. The gate mechanism again did more than score: its preflight caught, for the second run running, that the already-running app was serving a stale bundle — an 08:39 build predating plan 34.10-23's 11:17 rescope — so `build/` was cleared and rebuilt before any scored observation. Two further defects in the contract's own automated checks are recorded as **F-34.10-08** (P11's `^` anchor can never match vite's single-line minified CSS, proven by it returning `0` even for the file that does contain the rule; and its directory-wide glob spanned 14 accumulated build chunks). Neither altered a measured verdict, and both are recorded rather than fixed, per decision D-E. One sub-check remains unmeasured across all four runs — the gamepad focus-scroll regression — carried forward as a named permanent residual risk on REQ-34.10-06, never inferred. See `34.10-LIVE-GATE.md` §10 and §10.6.

Phase 34.11 (Library filtering — search, views, collections and cross-store facets). Minted 2026-08-09 during `/gsd-plan-phase 34.11` from `34.11-CONTEXT.md` D-01..D-35 + the checker-approved `34.11-UI-SPEC.md` (6/6 dimensions) + `34.11-RESEARCH.md` + `34.11-VALIDATION.md`. Each maps to Phase 34.11. **Three of ROADMAP.md's own "Known constraints" are superseded by measurement, not descoped:** the **genre facet is DROPPED** (D-13/D-14/D-15 — Epic contributes no genre data at all and Steam only for games the user has already opened, so shipping it would be half-populated); **filter-state persistence is decided, not open** (D-22..D-25); and the **runnability facet cannot render on Windows at all** — a full read of `src/common/types.ts` confirms `GameInfo` carries `is_mac_native`, `mac_arch` and `is_linux_native` but **no Windows-native field of any kind**, so D-12's own "hide rows you cannot compute" rule removes the entire group on that host. **Two decisions were resolved by the operator at plan time and are recorded as amendments in CONTEXT.md rather than silently applied:** `Fuse.js` fuzzy search STAYS (D-35 tagged `[informational — DEFERRED]`, D-33's "substring is today's shipped behaviour" premise falsified against `Library/index.tsx:601-616`), and the Runnability group is omitted entirely on Windows rather than rendering a degenerate all-pass row. **One defect found during UI-SPEC scouting and absent from every upstream artifact is minted as a requirement** (REQ-34.11-12): `.NavShell__tier2`'s `border-inline-end: 1px solid var(--divider)` uses a token defined in only 2 of 11 theme blocks — the third instance of this defect class, and the first to be pinned by a committed automated gate rather than a live human sweep.

- [x] **REQ-34.11-01**: The lower half of the Games tier-2 panel offers **exactly three collapsed facet groups — Store, Runnability and More filters — and no fourth group exists.** The Genre facet is dropped and nothing occupies the slot it vacated. On a Windows host the Runnability group renders **nothing at all**: no group header, no empty section, and explicitly not a degenerate all-pass row, because every one of its rows is uncomputable there. *(D-06, D-07, D-12 extension, D-16; Plan 34.11-07)*
- [x] **REQ-34.11-02**: Store and Runnability facets are **opt-in**: an empty selection means no constraint, never "everything excluded". Combination is **AND across facet kinds, OR within a kind** — `(GOG OR Steam) AND (Runs natively)` is what the panel means. *(D-01, D-03; Plans 34.11-01, 34.11-04, 34.11-05)*
- [x] **REQ-34.11-03**: The library filter pipeline exists as **named pure functions callable without React, jsdom or a DOM**. This is the Wave 0 prerequisite for every count and filter test in the phase: this repo sets `testEnvironment: 'node'` and installs neither `@testing-library/react` nor `react-test-renderer`, so filtering logic left inside a `useMemo` is untestable by construction. *(VALIDATION.md Wave 0; Plan 34.11-01)*
- [x] **REQ-34.11-04**: **One implementation produces both the grid and the facet counts**, so a count can never disagree with what the grid shows. The legacy stages are retired in the same change: `filterByPlatform` and the user-facing `crossoverRatingFilters` branch no longer run. *(D-09, D-10; Plans 34.11-01, 34.11-05)*
- [x] **REQ-34.11-05**: The panel offers **exactly four Views — `All games`, `Installed`, `Recently played`, `Favourites` — as single-select rows**, and choosing one deselects the previous one. The previously-combinable `showInstalledOnly` + `showFavourites` pair is no longer expressible. *(D-05; Plans 34.11-04, 34.11-06)*
- [x] **REQ-34.11-06**: **Filter state persistence is explicit per filter kind, not incidental.** Search text is session-only React state and is cleared on tab switch; every other filter survives a tab switch and a restart via `localStorage`. `Clear all` writes the cleared state through to storage, so what the chip row shows is what comes back next launch. *(D-22, D-23, D-24, D-25; Plan 34.11-04)*
- [x] **REQ-34.11-07**: A **legacy opt-out value found in `localStorage` is discarded, never translated** into an equivalent opt-in selection. Persisted state written by the pre-34.11 filter model is untrusted input: silently reinterpreting it would hand the user a filter set they never chose. *(D-01, D-02; Plan 34.11-04)*
- [x] **REQ-34.11-08**: Collections rows are the **verbatim output of `customCategories.listCategories()`** plus the literal `preset_uncategorized` pseudo-category — no migration, no derived collection, no second source of truth, and no sort/dedupe/case transform applied on the way through. Collections are single-select, management reuses the existing `CategoriesManager` dialog rather than rebuilding it, and the zero-collections case reuses the shipped `header.no_categories` string. Rule-based/smart collections are explicitly not built. *(D-17, D-19, D-20, D-21; Plan 34.11-06)*
- [x] **REQ-34.11-09**: Runnability tiers are derived using **`CrossoverBadge`'s exact existing bucketing**, including its undefined-vs-null distinction, so the facet and the per-game badge can never disagree. A 32-bit-only macOS build (`mac_arch: '32'`) counts as **playable via bottle** and is grouped with the bottle row, not with "won't run". *(D-09, D-10, D-11, D-16; Plans 34.11-01, 34.11-05)*
- [x] **REQ-34.11-10**: **A facet option's count is computed with every active filter EXCEPT that facet's own.** Ticking `GOG` leaves the other Store counts showing what they *would* yield rather than collapsing to 0 — this is the difference between counts that guide the next click and counts that lie. Counts use `font-variant-numeric: tabular-nums` and appear on facet options only, never on chips. *(D-28, D-29; Plans 34.11-01, 34.11-07)*
- [x] **REQ-34.11-11**: `NavItem`'s `elementType="button"` branch accepts a caller-supplied `className` and can render a selected state. Today that branch is `case 'button': return <button className="NavItem" onClick={onClick}>` with no `active`/`className` support at all, so a chosen View or Collection has **no way to read distinctly from an unchosen one**. The `NavLink` branch's existing `active`/`isActiveFallback` behaviour is unchanged and its five shipped tests stay green untouched. *(Plan 34.11-02, consumed by 34.11-06)*
- [x] **REQ-34.11-12**: **Three instances of the same theme-token defect class are fixed and pinned by a committed automated source gate** — the first time this class is caught by anything other than a live human sweep. CR-01: `--navbar-active` is undefined in `gruvbox_dark`. CR-02: `-webkit-app-region: drag` is missing from `.NavShell__navbar` with `no-drag` on its interactive children. Third instance, found during UI-SPEC scouting: `.NavShell__tier2`'s `border-inline-end` resolves `var(--divider)`, a token defined in only 2 of 11 theme blocks and absent from both mandatory stress-test themes. Source-side fixes and the pinning gate are done (Plan 34.11-03), but per that plan's own instruction this requirement is **not ticked from source alone** — CR-01 and the `--divider` fix are live-verifiable and CR-02 is source-verified-only forever (inert under WKWebView); plan 09 Task 3's three-theme live sweep is the adjudicator that closes this checkbox. *(D-31, D-32, UI-SPEC "New Finding"; Plan 34.11-03, closed by Plan 34.11-09)*
- [x] **REQ-34.11-13**: **Every non-default filter surfaces as a removable chip above the grid, including views**, so the user can always see why they are looking at 6 games instead of 214. A tri-state chip's `×` returns that filter to `off` and never cycles. The `×` is icon-only and therefore carries an interpolated accessible name (`Remove {{filterLabel}} filter`) — a bare `aria-label="Remove"` announces four identical controls when four chips are active and is not acceptable. *(D-25, D-26, D-27, D-28; Plan 34.11-08)*
- [x] **REQ-34.11-14**: The zero-result state **names the filters responsible** — e.g. `No games match Installed + GOG + "witcher"` — and carries an inline `Clear all filters` action. A generic "No results" message is a failure of this requirement, not an acceptable fallback. *(D-30; Plan 34.11-08)*
- [x] **REQ-34.11-15**: **Search keeps its existing `Fuse.js` fuzzy behaviour unchanged this phase, and facet counts are computed over the fuzzy-matched set.** This is a recorded operator decision, not an oversight: research falsified D-33's claim that substring matching is what ships today (`Library/index.tsx:601-616` runs `Fuse.js` at `threshold: 0.4`), and removing it was ruled out of scope. The consequence is therefore a specified behaviour rather than a defect — a count can include a title the user would not consider a match. *(D-33 amendment, D-35 `[informational — DEFERRED]`; Plans 34.11-01, 34.11-05)*
- [x] **REQ-34.11-16**: **The panel actually renders the phase's work.** Opening the Games tab shows search, then Views, then Collections, then the Store / Runnability / More filters groups, in that order, from a single mount point. `components/UI/CategoryFilter` and `components/UI/LibraryFilters` no longer exist on disk and nothing in `src/` imports either; `LibrarySearchBar` stays exactly where it is and is neither moved nor reimplemented. *(D-21, D-34, UI-SPEC Panel Structure; Plan 34.11-09)*
- [x] **REQ-34.11-17**: **The phase's own gates are green and non-vacuous.** `tier2Portal.test.ts`'s `CategoryFilter`/`LibraryFilters` import-order assertions — which go red the moment the retirement lands — are **replaced, never deleted or skipped**, and each replacement is observed FAILING against a known-bad input before being accepted, because an unmatchable grep passes vacuously. `pnpm lint-translations:gamelib` is green, the new `gamelib:` keys are confirmed present in `public/locales/en/gamelib.json` rather than assumed, and `meta/i18nGateScope.json` lists no path that does not exist on disk. A live multi-theme sweep is elicited **one question per theme** across `midnightMirage`, `gruvbox_dark` and `dracula` — a single combined question has produced a false pass on this project before. Gamepad focus-scroll, unmeasured across all four of Phase 34.10's gate runs, is **measured and recorded only**; fixing it is out of scope. *(D-18, VALIDATION.md Manual-Only rows; Plan 34.11-09)*

Phase 36 (Login-to-Steam crossfade and explicit login-in-flight mitigation). Minted 2026-08-20
during `/gsd-plan-phase 36` from ROADMAP.md's Phase 36 section and its four numbered obligations —
no `36-CONTEXT.md` exists for this phase. Each maps to Phase 36. Per `36-VALIDATION.md`'s own
Manual-Only Verifications table, REQ-36-02 and REQ-36-03 each carry a scored item on plan 36-03's
BLOCKING human gate (not yet run as of this authoring) and stay unticked until that gate PASSES
them — `disabled` here is a React prop consumed by a JS early return and cross-in-flight motion is
invisible to this project's `testEnvironment: 'node'` jest suite, so neither claim is provable by
source gate alone. REQ-36-01 and REQ-36-04 carry no live-gate row in `36-VALIDATION.md` and are
satisfied in full by plan 36-01's own automated verification.

- [x] **REQ-36-01**: The Steam sign-in surface is a co-mounted overlay on `/login`, not a sibling route. `loginweb/steam` is removed from the router; `SteamLogin` dismisses through a callback prop; zero `navigate(` calls remain in `SteamLogin/index.tsx`; all eight censused dismissal paths funnel through one handler. *(Plan 36-01)*
- [x] **REQ-36-02**: An explicit `loginInFlight` guard replaces the incidental navigation unmount. All six Runner tiles carry `disabled={oldMac || loginInFlight}`; `.loginContentWrapper` carries `pointer-events: none` and React-18 string-form `inert` while the Steam flow is open; the flag clears synchronously on every exit path. The guard is deliberately THREE layers, not four: no `tabIndex` and no `aria-hidden` are added, and this requirement must not be reworded to ask for either. A defensive `tabIndex={-1}` was locked and then retired on 2026-08-20 once the premise it rested on was disproved from source (the tiles are bare `<div onClick>` with no `tabIndex` and no `role`, already outside the tab order, and container-level `tabIndex={-1}` does not remove focusable descendants anyway); on the macOS 12.0-12.3 slice where `inert` predates the platform, MUI's Dialog focus trap is what holds. Plan 36-01 both builds and GATES this three-layer shape — its Task 4 carries an ABSENCE assertion that `Login/index.tsx` contains no `tabIndex` and no `aria-hidden` — so a four-layer reading of this requirement would fail the phase's own test suite. Source shape is built and gated by Plan 36-01; DISCHARGED 2026-08-20 by plan 36-03's live gate — items 5/7/9 (the other five tiles genuinely dead; the guard releases on both close and success) all scored PASS within that gate's PRECONDITION PASS / 10-of-10 verdict. *(Plan 36-01, source; live discharge Plan 36-03)*
- [x] **REQ-36-03**: Clicking the Steam tile plays one continuous motion: `.loginContentWrapper` slides up and out via a CSS transition while the Steam Dialog slides up in at the same 500ms duration, with `.loginBackground` remaining painted underneath throughout. CSS built and source-gated by Plan 36-01 (`loginCrossfade.test.ts`, 6/6 mutation-proven assertions); DISCHARGED 2026-08-20 by plan 36-03's live gate — items 1/2/3 (motion, overlap, and painted background, all invisible to a `testEnvironment: 'node'` source gate) scored PASS on a freshly-built `pnpm tauri:dev`. *(Plan 36-01, source; live discharge Plan 36-03)*
- [x] **REQ-36-04**: `loginInFlightUiReachability.test.tsx` is rewritten — header prose and assertions — to pin the explicit guard, carries an Epic/SIDLogin exception clause and a FILLED-SPECIMEN guard, and every assertion has a recorded mutation-proven RED observation. *(Plan 36-01)*
- [x] **REQ-36-05**: The threat register records T-34.4.2-39/-41's basis change from incidental unmount to explicit guard, with the WITHDRAWN live-discharge disposition explicitly unchanged, and with F-36-01 and F-36-02 entered as named accepted-and-open findings. *(Plan 36-02 — Fourteenth update, `34.4.2-PLATFORM-SCOPE.md` §5)*

Phase 37 (Steam defect cluster — depot decode failure, false-delisted games, install-error
reporting). Minted 2026-08-22 during `/gsd-plan-phase 37` from `37-CONTEXT.md` D-01..D-17 plus
`37-RESEARCH.md`'s decomposition table; ROADMAP.md's `REQ-37-01..07 to be minted during
/gsd-plan-phase 37` line is replaced by this block. **Six IDs, not seven** — 37-07 (the startup
filesystem orphan scan) was DROPPED by D-01 on measured signal ratio (1.2%), not deferred, so no
ID was minted for it. **ID-to-plan mapping is offset and that is deliberate**, because four
ROADMAP plan slots (37-01, 37-08, 37-09, 37-11) were already closed by live gates on 2026-08-22
before planning began and keep their slot numbers in the ROADMAP checklist:

| Requirement | Plan slot | Defect |
|-------------|-----------|--------|
| REQ-37-01 | 37-02 | Decode failure misreported as "servers dropped the connection" |
| REQ-37-02 | 37-03 | Nine owned games permanently flagged delisted and hidden |
| REQ-37-03 | 37-04 | Install-failed dialog renders an empty game title |
| REQ-37-04 | 37-05 | Abort-controller missing on terminal install failure |
| REQ-37-05 | 37-06 | Platform-precedence timestamp has no upper bound |
| REQ-37-06 | 37-10 | Apostrophe installdir rejected as hostile |

**REQ-37-02 supersedes LIB-07 and amends LIB-08** (both `[x]` complete, Phase 08.1). LIB-07 reads
"Steam's delisted signal drives the Library 'show non-available' filter — non-available means
delisted, not merely not-installed"; D-11 replaces that forced hide with an opt-in tri-state facet
defaulting to `off` (delisted games VISIBLE). LIB-08's greyed placeholder and disabled install
option both survive (D-14 keeps the install doors closed), but its "Game no longer available" copy
is replaced by "No store page" under a NEW i18n key (D-12). Those two boxes stay ticked as a
record of what Phase 08.1 shipped; this block is the authority on current behaviour.

**Phase-wide constraint (ROADMAP.md, restated so no requirement is read as licensing it): no
`Migration` may be used as a repair path.** `MigrationSystem` is dead code under Tauri —
`applyMigrations()` is wired only into Electron's `app.whenReady()` — so a new `Migration` ships as
a silent no-op. Repairs of already-written state self-heal at the READ boundary.

- [ ] **REQ-37-01**: A depot chunk that fails to DECODE is never reported to the user as "Steam servers dropped the connection". `depotErrors.ts`'s classifier stops branching on the `failed after \d+ attempts` term — which describes the *shape* of a failure, not its cause — and branches instead on the cause carried forward by `fetchChunk`'s exhaustion wrapper; genuine network exhaustion still matches on `ECONNRESET`/`ETIMEDOUT`/`CDN \d`. The classifier's return type widens from `{key, message}` to carry structured retryability/action so affordances come out of a field rather than out of translated prose, and an auth failure (no refresh token at plan-build) gets its own message plus a "Sign in to Steam" affordance instead of falling through to the generic branch. **The landmine research found must be fixed first or the signal can never fire on the path most real failures take:** `DepotDownloadFailure.error` is stringified at `depot.ts:2426` before it reaches `classifyDepotError`, silently discarding `.eresult`/`.code`. **Test that must fail first:** feed the classifier a retry-exhaustion message originating from `ChunkDecodeError` and assert it does NOT return `steam.download.error.connectionDropped`. *(D-06, D-07, D-08)*
- [ ] **REQ-37-02**: No owned, non-delisted-by-the-user game is hidden from the library by a flag nothing can clear. `is_delisted` is demoted from a forced hide to a user-driven filterable state; detection is CORRECT and unchanged and **no migration clears the stored flags** — all nine genuinely return `success: false` against a passing four-title control, so clearing them would record a falsehood. The filter is a tri-state row **inside the existing `FilterMoreGroup`**, states `off`/`only`/`hide` (neutral `off` = not filtering, no descriptor, no chip), adding one descriptor kind to `MORE_FILTER_KINDS` and to `describeActiveFilters` in the same change. Row label and card badge both read **"No store page"** under a NEW i18n key — changing the `t()` default argument is a silent no-op once the key exists. **Backend and frontend land in ONE change (D-15): `SteamGame.isGameAvailable()` returns `false` for a delisted game, which pushes it onto `nonAvailableGames` and hides it again through the first clause of the same OR — a frontend-only fix is not a partial fix, it traps Dead Island (91310) harder than doing nothing.** The new facet must NOT be routed through `nonAvailableGames`, whose single writer means "an INSTALLED game whose install_path went missing". Console mode lifts too (`ConsoleMode/selectors.ts` grid filter and `index.tsx`'s `activateGame` early return), and the two readers that go stale in the same change — `gameCount.ts`'s `!game.is_delisted` term and `hooks/constants.ts`'s "keeps hiding it regardless" doc comment — are corrected alongside. Install doors (`steamInstallOptionsEntry.ts`) stay CLOSED (D-14). **A green suite does not close this**: the live case is Dead Island (91310), installed on this machine, visible and launchable at default filter settings. *(D-10..D-16)*
- [ ] **REQ-37-03**: A failed Steam install never renders "The installation of  failed". `downloadmanager/utils.ts`'s `title` falls back to `appName` (the appid) rather than an empty string, matching the fallback `error` already has. The root cause research identified — `SteamGame.getGameInfo()` returns `{}` on an async cache miss, a gap unique to Steam because GOG/Legendary carry a synchronous `loadFile()` fallback Steam lacks — is **investigated and recorded, not gated** (D-09); if closing it widens the phase it becomes a filed todo rather than holding this requirement open. *(D-09)*
- [ ] **REQ-37-04**: A terminal Steam install failure does not log `Aborting not possible. Could not find a matching abort controller`. Research resolved the cause as a registration-timing gap, not a teardown race: `createAbortController` registers only inside `runNativeDepotDownload`, but a pre-download step in `SteamGame.install()`/`installNative` can throw first and reach `utils.ts`'s unconditional `callAbortController` with nothing ever registered. **User-initiated cancel is confirmed NOT affected** — `downloadqueue.ts` calls abort synchronously once a live download is already known to exist — and the fix must not silence the log for the case where a real controller is genuinely missing. *(Claude's Discretion; recorded first check discharged by research)*
- [ ] **REQ-37-05**: `resolvePlatformWrite` bounds `platformsCapturedAt` from **above** as well as below, and validates the **incoming** `capturedAt` the same way it validates the existing one. Today `existingCapturedAt` has only a `Number.isFinite` lower-bound check and incoming has none, so a clock-skewed or corrupted stamp outranks every later write for that appId permanently with no repair path (WR-02, IN-01). Exposure is a previously-persisted corrupted value, since both live call sites pass `Date.now()`. **This must not be "fixed" by ranking the two sources** — freshest-write-wins was chosen deliberately over "appdetails always wins"/"PICS always wins". *(D-17)*
- [ ] **REQ-37-06**: A legitimate PICS `installdir` containing ordinary filename punctuation — the apostrophe in `Sid Meier's Civilization V` — is used as-is rather than being logged "hostile" and silently replaced with `app_<id>`, which lands the game where Steam will never adopt it. `sanitizeInstalldir` decides acceptability by **containment validation against the resolved install root** (mirroring `resolveContainedPath`) **plus** a narrow explicit denylist — path separators, `..`, leading/trailing dots, control characters — kept as defense in depth. **Do not simply widen the `SAFE_INSTALLDIR` character class**: the check exists to stop a hostile PICS response escaping the install root, and a widening without a RED traversal test trades this defect for a worse one. Both callers keep the single shared funnel. The two fallback triggers **split**: a containment violation is a security event that **ABORTS the install** with a message naming the rejected value and no silent fallback write, while an absent/unresolved installdir keeps the `app_<id>` fallback but logs WARNING and surfaces in the install result — closing the `!candidate` branch's current "no log at all" gap that actually produced `app_259130`. The three existing mis-named installs (`app_8930`/`app_25900`/`app_257350`, 16.2 GB) are **left alone** (D-05). *(D-02, D-03, D-04, D-05)*

Phase 34.16 (macOS runner onedir x64 CI leg — publish the workflow to the default branch, source and
digest-verify darwin archives, and extend the runner-bundle guard beyond arm64). Minted 2026-08-24
during `/gsd-plan-phase 34.16` from `34.16-CONTEXT.md` D-01..D-12 + `34.16-RESEARCH.md` §Phase
Requirements + `34.16-VALIDATION.md`'s per-requirement test map. **Four new IDs, alongside three
inherited ones.** REQ-34.9-02, REQ-34.9-03 and REQ-34.9-04 are **re-scoped to this phase** rather
than re-minted — they keep their original IDs and their `deferred-items.md` items 1/2/3 lineage.
Ledger items **12**, **13** and **18 / C2-05** never had a REQ of their own, so REQ-34.16-01, -02 and
-03 are minted for them here; REQ-34.16-04 covers the `pin:runner-digests` tooling D-10/D-11 require.

ROADMAP.md's Phase 34.16 line `REQ-34.9-02, REQ-34.9-03, REQ-34.9-04 (inherited, re-scoped to this
phase; mint new REQ-34.16-* IDs at plan time for items 12, 13 and 18, which have no REQ of their
own)` is replaced by this block.

- [ ] **REQ-34.16-01**: The wired `verify:runner-bundle` guard covers **x64 as well as arm64**. `dist:mac` and `release:mac` each carry two literal, sequential `pnpm verify:runner-bundle build --arch=<X>` invocations, `&&`-chained before `electron-builder`, so each failure names its own arch (D-05; the tree-derived variant was rejected for passing vacuously against an empty or relaid-out tree). Closes `deferred-items.md` item **12**. **The coverage consequence stays UNPROVEN until observed:** whether a real x64 darwin onedir tree passes the guard's framework-structure checks has never been seen, because no x64 onedir tree existed anywhere until this phase. `34.16-LIVE-GATE.md` Scored Item 5 **branches** on the result (CLEAN PASS / BUILD BUG / GUARD BUG) rather than predicting it, and D-07's bar requires a local RED on a real x64 tree naming `arch=x64` — CI green is execution evidence only.
- [ ] **REQ-34.16-02**: That guard is **actually exercised in CI**, not merely wired into the live CI definition. Ledger item **13**'s mechanism: `.github/actions/install-deps`'s last step is `pnpm download-helper-binaries`, which throws on the six `PENDING-CI-PUBLISH` sentinels, so the macOS leg has never reached `pnpm dist:mac` at all. Real digests remove that block and the guard runs with **no new CI wiring** (D-06 — it stays npm-script-level so it cannot drift away from the build it protects). Verified by `34.16-LIVE-GATE.md` Scored Item 6: two distinct per-arch `verify:runner-bundle` banners in a real macOS run log, both preceding any `electron-builder  version=` line, plus a `git diff` proving no workflow step was added.
- [ ] **REQ-34.16-03**: An **unverified x64 macOS build cannot reach the auto-update channel**. Closes `deferred-items.md` item **18 / C2-05**, whose two mechanisms must never be collapsed into one: `build-base.yml:48` runs `pnpm dist:mac --x64 --arm64 **--publish=never**` (both arches built, one verified, nothing published), while the auto-publish arm is `release:mac` (`package.json:46`), a script a **human** runs, chaining `-p always` into the feed `electron-updater` consumes. D-05's second invocation lands in `release:mac`, which is item 18's own named precondition; `-p always` deliberately stays (D-08 — `--publish=never` rewrites a release ritual Phase 35's Electron cutover deletes). **Per D-09 this closure is UNPINNED against regression, deliberately and with the consequence accepted:** the existing pin at `meta/__tests__/verifyRunnerBundle.test.ts:605-622` asserts only `indexOf('pnpm verify:runner-bundle') < indexOf('electron-builder')` and stays **green if a future edit deletes the x64 invocation**. No document may describe this closure as "pinned".
- [ ] **REQ-34.16-04**: `pnpm pin:runner-digests` exists and is **fail-closed**. It fetches `SHA256SUMS-{x64,arm64}` and `BUILD-MANIFEST-{x64,arm64}.json` from the `runners-onedir-macos` rolling release over hardcoded, unparameterisable repo/tag constants, validates every line against `^[0-9a-f]{64}  \S+$`, and rewrites `meta/runnersOnedirDigests.json`'s six digests plus a new top-level `runId` in a single write — or writes nothing at all (D-10). It is structurally incapable of inventing, renaming or dropping a digest key, because it derives its expected filename set from the existing keys. The `runId` is a genuine prerequisite, not a wording detail: `writeBuildManifest()` carried **no run-id field of any kind** before this phase, so D-10 was not literally satisfiable. `runId` is a top-level sibling of `layout`/`digests` and is structurally invisible to `computeLayoutMarker()`, so it forces no spurious global re-download — proven behaviourally through `darwinLayoutMarker()`, not by source inspection. D-11 quotes the pinned run in the digest-mismatch throw so `--clobber` drift says *which run to re-pin from*, without weakening the refusal to write anything under `public/bin`.

Phase 34.17 (`PathSelectionBox` input commit — Enter-to-commit and the unconfirmed paste failure).
Minted 2026-08-25 during `/gsd-plan-phase 34.17` from ROADMAP.md's `### Phase 34.17` section (this
phase has **no CONTEXT.md** — that ROADMAP section is the authoritative scope document) plus
`34.17-RESEARCH.md` § Candidate Requirements, `34.17-PATTERNS.md` and `34.17-VALIDATION.md`.
ROADMAP.md's `**Requirements**: NONE inherited — item 8 never had a REQ of its own. Mint
`REQ-34.17-*` IDs at plan time.` line is replaced by this block.

**The four IDs are deliberately not homogeneous.** `deferred-items.md` item 8 carries **two
unrelated defects**, and the ROADMAP forbids planning them as one. REQ-34.17-01/-02/-03 are Half A
— fix obligations, provable from source at HEAD. **REQ-34.17-04 is Half B and is a
reproduction-or-falsification obligation, NOT a fix obligation.** It is discharged by recorded
evidence either way, and a run that fails to reproduce the defect closes it as VERIFIED-ABSENT.
Any future reader who re-reads REQ-34.17-04 as "fix the paste bug" has misread it; the ROADMAP's
own words are that "a fix aimed at an unreproduced cause is how this project has repeatedly
shipped gates that measure the wrong property."

**Automated coverage is partial by contract, not by omission.** `34.17-VALIDATION.md` establishes
that REQ-34.17-01 and -02 are unit-provable in the no-DOM `Frontend` jest project, while
REQ-34.17-03 (visual affordance) and REQ-34.17-04 (native paste delivery) are **manual-only** and
close on human-captured evidence. A green jest run must never be cited as covering -03 or -04.

- [x] **REQ-34.17-01** (Half A, fix): Pressing Enter while focused in any `PathSelectionBox` field commits the currently-typed value through the same `onPathChange` contract `onBlur` already uses, without the field losing focus. Wired through `TextInputWithIconField`'s **already-declared** `onKeyDown` prop (`TextInputWithIconField/index.tsx:17`), which already reaches the real DOM `<input>` via `TextInputField`'s prop spread at `TextInputField/index.tsx:65` — **no interface widening is required and any widening diff means the fix landed at the wrong layer**. Matches the existing house idiom at `SteamGridDBPicker/index.tsx:168-172` (`e.key === 'Enter'` only; the `|| e.key === ' '` variant found in ConsoleMode is the ARIA button-activation idiom and is wrong here, because Space is a legal path character). Proven by test, not by prop presence: the gate must **invoke** the handler and must include a **non-Enter negative case**, because a handler that commits on every keystroke is a worse defect than the one being fixed. Plan `34.17-01`.
- [x] **REQ-34.17-02** (Half A, fix): An Enter commit immediately followed by a blur carrying the same, unchanged value does not double-fire a non-idempotent side effect. Concretely: `EgsSettings.tsx`'s `PathSelectionBox` — the **one** call site of 13 whose `onPathChange` is not guard-idempotent, because `handleSync` calls `window.api.egsSync` with no debounce — must not spawn `legendary egl-sync` twice for one user edit. The guard lives **inside `PathSelectionBox`**, generalising to all 13 callers at once; `EgsSettings.tsx` is not edited. **Planning correction to `34.17-RESEARCH.md`, recorded because it changes the implementation:** research recommended option (b), comparing the incoming value against the already-committed `path` prop, on its own. That is insufficient at the one site it exists for — `egsPath` is only updated after the IPC resolves, so `path` is stale for the whole duration of a commit and the comparison never fires. The shipped guard is therefore two-part: (b) plus a one-shot, value-scoped suppression of the blur that immediately follows an Enter commit of the same string. One-shot and value-scoped, not a blanket blur suppression, so a genuine edit made between the Enter and the blur still commits and a retry after a rejected commit still fires. Plan `34.17-01`.
- [ ] **REQ-34.17-03** (Half A, fix, affordance clause): After a commit, the user receives a visible, non-blocking confirmation that the edit was saved, and while the edit buffer diverges from the committed path the field says so. **`34.17-PATTERNS.md`'s survey found no reusable "field committed" affordance in this codebase** — `TextInputField`'s `warning` slot is a permanent hint tied to value truthiness, `HumbleExpiryToast` is a persistent global-state banner, and every `aria-live` region lives in ConsoleMode, a screen family `PathSelectionBox` never renders in. **No toast or snackbar library exists in this app**, independently recorded in two in-repo comments (`HumbleExpiryToast/index.tsx:14-20`, `SteamBottleSetup.tsx:17`), so this is a genuinely new, deliberately minimal local element rather than the reuse of a pattern. Strings land in `public/locales/en/gamelib.json`, never `translation.json`. **Closure is manual-only by contract:** asserting a CSS class name appears in a mock render tree passes even when the class has no visible effect — this project has already shipped exactly that failure — so the artifact of record is a human-captured screenshot in one light and one dark theme. Plan `34.17-02`.
- [ ] **REQ-34.17-04** (Half B, **reproduction-or-falsification, not a fix**): The paste symptom recorded on 2026-08-11 — a repeating unrenderable glyph instead of the clipboard text — either reproduces on a real macOS Tauri host with evidence attached, or is confirmed absent and closed **VERIFIED-ABSENT** with the same evidence standard. **Both outcomes discharge this requirement.** The ledger's own suspected cause, `navigator.clipboard` no-opping under the Tauri host, is recorded here as **mechanically implausible**: that standing note concerns programmatic `writeText()`/`readText()`, Phase 34.3 (`44232d3e`) migrated every such call site onto `window.api.clipboard*`, zero `navigator.clipboard` call sites remain, and no code path connects that API to a native Cmd+V into a plain `<input>`. A more plausible but still unconfirmed candidate is the tao/wry key-equivalent delivery gap already fixed **once** in `src-tauri/src/main.rs:2363-2454` — for the pristine Epic login child window only, with that fix's own comment stating it deliberately leaves every other window unchanged, so the primary window is uncovered. Note the symptoms differ: that candidate predicts Cmd+V does *nothing*, while the ledger reports a glyph. The protocol discriminates rather than assuming, via two controls on one screen — the fully uncontrolled `#search` input (host-level discriminator) and `#steamgriddb-api-key`, a `TextInputField` that is not a `PathSelectionBox` (component-layer discriminator). **Scope fence, binding:** if the cause is host-level, the terminal action is to mint a new item and STOP — this phase does not own a general clipboard shim and no file under `src-tauri/` may be touched for it. Plan `34.17-03`.

**D-XX -> REQ mapping (Phase 34.4.1):** D-01 -> REQ-34.4.1-01/03/09 · D-02 (resolved by research → Rust arms) -> REQ-34.4.1-01/02 · D-03 (declaration discipline; bad case now unlikely post-spike) -> REQ-34.4.1-04/10 · D-04 -> REQ-34.4.1-08 · D-05 -> REQ-34.4.1-07 · D-06 -> REQ-34.4.1-07 · D-07 -> REQ-34.4.1-05 · D-08 -> REQ-34.4.1-05/06 · D-09 -> REQ-34.4.1-12/13 · D-10 -> REQ-34.4.1-10/12 · hard-constraint (additive/reversible BEHAVIORAL + SEAM Invariants A/B) -> REQ-34.4.1-11. **D-11 is a PRE-PLANNING PRECONDITION, not a requirement** — it demanded an executed spike before any plan could be written, and spikes 013/014a/014b/015 discharged it on 2026-07-27 before minting; it is recorded in the minting rationale rather than minted, and its evidence is what resolves D-02 and de-risks D-03. The Claude's-Discretion points shape requirements rather than becoming them: the ownership shape folds into REQ-34.4.1-01, the stale-`isTauri()`-guard and bare-import sweeps into REQ-34.4.1-11, Linux/Windows safe-degradation into REQ-34.4.1-13, the `electronStub.net.request` retire-or-keep call into REQ-34.4.1-05, the curated-import rule into REQ-34.4.1-02/05/06 (**`humble/ipc_handler.ts` MUST NOT be side-effect-imported — it registers the 15 channels 34.4 already ported**), send-vs-handle discipline into REQ-34.4.1-03, and `humbleGetLoginUserAgent`'s pure-function status into REQ-34.4.1-04. Research resolved the one open architectural question (D-02) in favour of Rust-arms-driven-by-the-sidecar and corrected the `<webview>` element's line citation (`:488-503`, not `:467-480`) inherited from ROADMAP.md into CONTEXT.md.

**D-XX -> REQ mapping (Phase 34.8):** D-01 -> REQ-34.8-01 · D-02 -> REQ-34.8-02 · D-03 -> REQ-34.8-03 · D-04 -> REQ-34.8-01 · D-05 -> REQ-34.8-04 · D-06 -> REQ-34.8-05 · D-07 -> REQ-34.8-06/REQ-34.8-11 · D-08/D-09/D-10/D-11 -> REQ-34.8-15 · D-12 -> REQ-34.8-08/REQ-34.8-09 · D-13 -> REQ-34.8-07 · D-14 -> REQ-34.8-07/REQ-34.8-08/REQ-34.8-13 · D-15 -> REQ-34.8-14 · D-16 -> REQ-34.8-07/REQ-34.8-08/REQ-34.8-09 · D-17 -> REQ-34.8-10/REQ-34.8-17 · D-18 -> REQ-34.8-10 · D-19 -> REQ-34.8-11/REQ-34.8-12/REQ-34.8-13 · D-20 -> REQ-34.8-16 · D-21 -> REQ-34.8-02 · D-22 -> REQ-34.8-13. The four **negative** decisions are minted as requirements rather than left implicit, because a deliberate non-action that is not written down reads as an omission at verification time: D-06 (fork keys stay in the upstream catalogs — migrating would require the very deletions REQ-34.8-04 blocks), D-20 (no Weblate project), D-21 (brand names glossaried, not `t()`-wrapped — so ConsoleMode/LogSettings/StoreSearch need zero code change) and D-22 (PlatformSupport.tsx dropped as a measurement-superseded audit false positive). The five Claude's-Discretion points shape requirements rather than becoming them: namespace registration folds into REQ-34.8-01 (resolved: lazy loading, no `ns` array, but a live both-runtime check is still required because the Tauri `publicDir` divergence has recurred four times), the `gamelib` key-naming convention into REQ-34.8-01, the glossary's physical location and format into REQ-34.8-02, the CI host into REQ-34.8-09 (resolved: `test.yml` via the existing `meta` jest project — zero new YAML), and the definition of "user-facing" into REQ-34.8-07 (resolved with a measured INCLUDE/EXCLUDE list). 34.8-RESEARCH.md Assumption A3 (the full retrofit target set was NOT enumerated during research) is discharged inside REQ-34.8-11 by the gate's own audit-mode run plus a blocking developer decision if the backlog exceeds 40 violations or 12 files — never by a silent scope cut.

**D-XX -> REQ mapping (Phase 34.10):** D-01 -> REQ-34.10-03 · D-02 -> REQ-34.10-04 · D-03 -> REQ-34.10-05 · D-04 -> REQ-34.10-06/REQ-34.10-02 · D-05 -> REQ-34.10-07 · D-06 -> REQ-34.10-08 · D-07 -> REQ-34.10-08 · D-08 -> REQ-34.10-09 · D-09 -> REQ-34.10-09 · D-10 -> REQ-34.10-10 · D-11 -> REQ-34.10-12 · D-12 -> REQ-34.10-11 · D-13 -> REQ-34.10-13 · whole-phase shell swap -> REQ-34.10-01 · UI-SPEC Locked Inputs + OQ5/OQ6 defaults -> REQ-34.10-02 · UI-SPEC Copywriting Contract + RESEARCH Trap 3 -> REQ-34.10-14 · VALIDATION.md no-jsdom hard constraint + RESEARCH Testing Impact -> REQ-34.10-15 · VALIDATION.md Manual-Only rows -> REQ-34.10-16. **Three requirements are minted for traps rather than for features**, because each one survives a green test suite: REQ-34.10-04 (the `.Sidebar` drag fallback is the ACTIVE WKWebView path and its three pinning fixtures are test-controlled fiction), REQ-34.10-14 (`meta/i18nGateScope.json` is a static snapshot, so new files are invisible to the blocking gate until regenerated) and REQ-34.10-12 (`HeroicVersion`'s cache-clear and startup changelog are mount-triggered, so its visual placement must not become its mount condition — this resolves RESEARCH Open Question 1, which no CONTEXT decision answered). **One requirement is minted for a trap found at planning time and present in no upstream artifact**: REQ-34.10-09's portal, forced by `LibraryContext`'s `initialContext` full set of no-op setters — a `<Header>` rendered in the shell instead of inside `Library`'s provider would show filter controls that look right and do nothing, with no error and no failing test. **Two negative decisions are minted rather than left implicit**: REQ-34.10-05 (Electron cosmetic divergence recorded, not fixed) and REQ-34.10-13 (the tour is turned off and its rework deferred to its own phase). The two UI-SPEC Open Questions left at their recommended defaults (OQ5 icon-only wordmark, OQ6 no search icon / no avatar) shape REQ-34.10-02 rather than becoming requirements; UI-SPEC OQ1/OQ2/OQ3/OQ4 are superseded by D-01/D-12/D-11/D-13 respectively. RESEARCH Open Question 3 (console mode) is resolved inside REQ-34.10-01 in favour of no shell in console mode, matching today's no-sidebar precedent.

The four IDs preserve the ROADMAP's mandatory Half A / Half B split: REQ-34.17-01/-02/-03 are fix obligations, **REQ-34.17-04 is a reproduction-or-falsification obligation and closes on evidence either way**. Two planning findings are recorded in the requirement text rather than left in a plan: (1) `34.17-RESEARCH.md`'s recommended double-commit guard — comparing against the `path` prop — is **insufficient at the only call site it exists for**, because `EgsSettings.tsx`'s `egsPath` is stale for the duration of a commit, so REQ-34.17-02 ships a two-part guard; (2) the ledger's suspected paste cause (`navigator.clipboard`) is **mechanically implausible** — Phase 34.3 removed every such call site and no code path connects that API to a native Cmd+V — while a real, precedented candidate (the tao/wry key-equivalent gap fixed once at `src-tauri/src/main.rs:2363-2454` for the Epic login child window only) predicts a *different symptom* than the one reported, which is itself a discriminator. The UI safety gate was skipped by developer decision (`--skip-ui`); no UI-SPEC exists and none is required.*
